tamiḻ paḻamoḻikaḷ tokuppum āṅkila moḻipeyarppum, āciriyar percivāl, pākam 2 (3001- 6000) Tamil Proverbs with Their Translation by Rev. P. Percival . part 2 (second 3000)

Source:
Tamil Proverbs with their English Translation
contining upwards of Six thousand proverbs
By The Rev. P. Percival,
Chaplin, Madras Military Female Orphan Asylum:
Author of the "Land of the Veda" etc.
Second Edition, 1874
Madras: Printed and Published at the Dinavartamani Press,
Little Bourne, Mylapore
--------------

tamiḻ paḻamoḻikaḷ tokuppum āṅkila moḻipeyarppum āciriyar percivāl, pākam 2 (3001- 6000) Tamil Proverbs with Their Translation by Rev. Percival

3001. kōṇik kōṇik kōṭi koṭuppatilum kōṇāmaṟ kāṇikoṭuppatu nallatu.
It is better to bestow a small gift freely than lacs with a wry face.
3002. kōttiram aṟintu peṇ koṭu, pāttiram aṟintu picci iṭu.
Having ascertined the character of the family give your dughter
in marriage, and knowing the worthiness of the applicant give alms.
3003. kōttiravīṉaṉ cāttiram pārppāṉ.
One of low birth consults a fortune-teller.
3004. kōpam illāta turiyum campaḷam illāta cēvakaṉum.
A master without anger, a servant without wages.
3005. kōpam pāpam, nittiric catturu.
Anger is sin, sleep is an enemy.
3006. kōpam caṇṭāḷam.
Anger ends in cruelty.
3007. kōpam aṟṟāṟ kurōtam aṟum.
When anger ceases, revenge ceases.
3008. kōpam uḷḷa iṭattil kuṇam uṇṭu.
Where there is anger, there may be excellent qualities.
3009. kōpi kutirimēl kaṭivāḷam illāṉ.
The irascible is like a man on horseback without a bridle.
3010. kōpuram tāṅkipōl naṭakkiṟāṉ.
He walks as if supporting a tower.
3011. kōpuram tāṅkiya pūtam pōl cumakkiṟāṉ.
He carries like a goblin that bears a tower.
3012. kōmuṭṭip puttikku mōcam illi.
The foresight of a komutti-merchant-never fails.
3013. kōyiliyum kuḷattiyum aṭuttu irukkavēṇṭum.
One should reside near a temple and a tank.
3014. kōyil illā ūrilē kuṭiyirukkalāmā?
May one dwell in a village in which there is no temple?
3015. kōyil maṇiyam eṉṟu kūppiṭṭāl pōtum.
It is enough to be called the manager of the temple.
3016. kōyiṟ pūṉi tēvarukku añcumā?
Will the temple cat reverence the deity ?
3017. kōyiṟ pūṉikkup payam ēṉ?
Why should a temple cat fear ?
3018. kōrakkar vittiyam kuṇattukku ēṟkumā?
Will the medical work by Korakkar assist one to find out the symptoms of
disease ?
3019. kōri kuṭiyik keṭukkum.
Course grass is ruinous to the cultivator.
3020. kōrik kiḻaṅku oru vēḷikku utavum.
Even the root of the kori grass will be of use sometime or other.
3021. kōl āṭak kuraṅku āṭum, atupōla maṉam āṭum.
As the staff moves the monkey moves, in like manner the mind moves.
3022. kōl iḻanta kuruṭaṉ pōla.
Like a blind man who has lost his staff.
3023. kōl eṭutta piḷḷi kuruṭṭuppiḷḷi.
The child that handles a stick is in danger of becoming blind.
3024. kōvukku aḻaku ceṅkōl muṟimi.
A sceptre of justice is the beauty of royalty.
3025. kōḻi aṭikkiṟataṟkuk kuṟuntaṭi vēṇṭumā?
Is a club needed to kill a fowl?
3026. kōḻi kaḷavupōka āṭu veṭṭip poṅkal iṭukiṟatā?
Do you attempt to recover a stolen fowl by sacrificial offerings?
3027. kōḻi kūvi viṭikiṟatā nāy kulittu viṭikiṟatā?
Does the day dawn at the crowing of a cock, or at the barking of a dog?
3028. kōḻikkuñcukkup pāl koṭuttatu pōla.
As one fed a chicken with milk.
3029. kōḻik kāyccalum kuṇṭaliṉi kāyccalum viṭātu.
The fever of fowls and the jealousy of a spiteful woman have no remedy.
3030. kōḻikkaṟi eṉṟatum koṇṭāṭik koṇṭatum
kīrittaṇṭāṇam aṭā cuppā kīrittaṇṭāṇam aṭā.
All that ado about the fowl curry, Subba, ends in a mess of greens.
3031. kōḻi ciṟakāl kuñcukaḷik kāppatupōla.
As a hen keeps her chickens under her wings.
3032. kōḻi tiruṭiyum kūṭa aḻukiṟāḷ.
Having herself stolen the fowl she weeps with the owner on account of
its loss.
3033. kōḻi tiṉṟa kaḷḷaṉum kūṭa niṉṟu ulāvukiṟāṉ.
The thief who has stolen the fowl, walks about with the owner in search
of it.
3034. kōḻi pōṉatum allāmal kuralum pōccutu.
Not only is the fowl gone, but her voice also is gone by calling it.
3035. kōḻi mitittuk kuñcu muṭam ākumā?
Will a chicken become lame if the mother hen treads on it?
3036. kōḻi muṭattukkuk kaṭā veṭṭip pali iṭṭatu pōl.
Like sacrificing a sheep for the recovery of a lame fowl.
3037. kōḻi muṭṭiyil mayir piṭuṅkalāmā?
Can hir be plucked off a hen's egg?
3038. kōḻiyik kēṭṭō āṇam kāyccukiṟatu ?
Is the fowl to be consulted when it is to be prepared for the table?
3039. kōḷukku muntēṉ kūḻukkup pintēṉ.
Be not first at slander nor last at meals.
3040. kōḷum collik kumpiṭuvāṉēṉ?
Why professions of respect regarding one about whom you are always
telling tales ?
3041. kucikattu oḷiyil evarkkum oḷirum.
Near the candle-stick it will be clear to all.
3042. keḷavi uṭiyār kālit toṭu.
Touch the feet of those with whom you have aught to do.
3043. keḷavi karutēṉ.
Be mindful of your business.
3044. keḷavic collil evarkkum paki.
Abusive words will create enmity.
3045. kuvippaṭṭāl kāyattil oru muḻam nīḷumā?
Can anxiety add length to one's body?
3046. keḷaviyatillāṉ tivviya collāṉ.
He who has no concern will not say any thing favourable.
3047. ṅappaṉ piṟantatu veḷḷimali , ṅāyi piṟantatu poṉmali.
My father was born in a silver mountin, and my mother was born in a
golden mountin.
3048. ṅap pōl vaḷi.
Bend yourself like the letter ṅa-be humble.
3049. cakatiyilē kalli viṭṭu eṟintāl taṉ tuṇi eṉṟum acalār tuṇi eṉṟum pārātu
.
If one throws stones into mud, his own cloth and those of other will be
spattered.
3050. cakalaṉ uṟavil cāṇ koṭi pañcamā?
Is there such a dearth of span-long-creepers that you are obliged to
stay at your sister's husband's house?
3051. cakalamum kaṟṟavaṉṟaṉṉic cārntiru.
Associate with a person who is versed in every thing.
3052. cakala tēcattu vārttiyum kaṟṟukkoḷḷukiṟatē yāvarukkum nallatu.
To study the different dialects of each country is useful to all.
3053. cakōtaraṉ uḷḷavaṉ paṭikku añcāṉ.
He who has a brother does not fear to fight.
3054. cakkuc cakku eṉṟu pākkut tiṉpāṉ capimecca,
vīṭṭilē vantu kaṭivāyi nakkuvāṉ peṇṭukaḷ meycca.
When out he chews betel to attract public notice, when he returns home
he licks the corners of his mouth to secure the admirationof women.
3055. caṅkaṉum puṅkaṉum canniyācikku utaviyā?
Are fools and blockheads of service to the religious mendicant ?
3056. caṅkilē vārttāl tīrttam, caṭṭiyilē vārttāl taṇṇīr.
If poured into a conch, water becomes sacred, if into a chatty,it is
what it is.
3057. caṅku āyiram koṇṭu kācikkip pōṉālum taṉ pāvam taṉṉōṭē.
Though one carries a thousand conchs to Benares, his sin sticks to him.
3058. caṅku vāyum āṇṭi vāyum.
The mouth of the conch, the mouth of the religious mendicant.
3059. caṅkōcam viṭṭāl caṅkiyum illi.
When deprived of modesty, there will be no sense of honour.
3060. caṭit tampirāṉ cāṟṟukku aḻukiṟāṉām, liṅkam pañca amirtattiṟku aḻukiṟatām.
It is sid that the religious mendicant with matted hir cries for
pepper water, and the linga he worships cries for the five delicacies,
viz., milk, curds, ghee, sugar and
honey.
3061. caṭit tampirāṉ cātam eṉkiṟapōtu moṭṭit tampirāṉukku mōr eṅkē kiṭikkum.
When a religious mendicant with matted hir finds it difficult to obtin
rice, where will the bald headed obtin butter milk.
3062. caṭit tampirāṉ taviṭṭukku aḻukiṟapōtu liṅkam paramāṉantattukku aḻukiṟatā?
When a religious mendicant is crying for bran, will the linga ask for
delicacies?
3063. caṭiyip piṭittu iḻuttāl canniyāci kiṭṭa varuvāṉ.
If dragged by his matted hir, the religious mendicant may be brought
near one.
3064. caṭṭi cuṭṭatum ki viṭṭatum.
The chatty burnt, the hand left it.
3065. caṭṭuvam kaṟiccuvi aṟiyumō?
Can a ladle appreciate the flavour of curry?
3066. caṇappaṉ vīṭṭuk kōḻi, tāṉē vilaṅku pūṭṭik koṇṭatu pōla.
As a flax-dresser's fowl fettered itself.
3067. caṇam pittam caṇam vātam.
One moment he is bilious and the next rheumatic.
3068. caṇṭamārutattiṟku etirppaṭṭa carukupōla.
Like dry leaves before a strong wind.
3069. caṇṭikku ēṟṟa miṇṭaṉ.
A stubborn person well suited to the self-willed.
3070. caṇṭi mukattilē uṟavā?
Is relationship recognised in a battle field ?
3071. caṇṇi aṇṇāmali eṉṟu peyar iṭuvāḷ.
She will call him Annamali the gormandizer.
3072. catakōṭi caṅkattilē moṭṭit tātaṉik kaṇṭāyā eṉkiṟatu pōla.
Like asking one in a crowd, if the bald headed devotee has been seen.
3073. caturakkaḷḷiyil akil uṇṭākum.
The eaglewood-acquilla grows with the prickly pear.
3074. catik kaṇṭu katti nāṭavēṇṭum.
The surgeon's knife must be judiciously applied.
3075. cati illāmaṟ katti nāṭumā?
Will the knife operate where there is no flesh ?
3076. cati uḷḷa iṭattilē katti nāṭum.
The surgeon's knife seeks the fleshy parts.
3077. catta mēkaṅkaḷum kūṭi neruppu maḻi peytāṟpōla.
As if seven clouds simultaneously rined fire.
3078. cattirattuk kūḻukku nāyakkar appaṇiyā?
Is the order of the Naidu required in order to procure gruel at the
choultry?
3079. cattirattup pāṭṭukkut tiruppāṭṭu mēlā?
Is a divine hymn superior to that sung in a choultry?
3080. cattirattilē pōcaṉam, maṭattilē nittiri.
Eating in a choultry and sleeping in a monastery.
3081. cattiyattilē cāmi cāṭci eṉkiṟa cattiyam peritu.
To say God is the witness of what one testifies, is the highest of all
forms of oath.
3082. cattiyattiṟku illāta piḷḷi tukkappaṭṭappōtu aḻap pōkiṟatā ?
Will the child that has no regard for truth go to weep with one when in
sorrow?
3083. cattiyam naṉṉili , cāvit tiṉam niṉi.
Truthfulness is the best condition, dily think of death.
3084. cattiyamē vellum acattiyamē kollum.
Truth previls, falsehood kills.
3085. cattiyamē kollum cattiyamē vellum.
Truth kills falsehood, truth previls.
3086. cattiya vācakaṉ camasta caṟkuṇaṉ.
The truthful man has all other virtues.
3087. cattirattuc cāppāṭṭukkut talattu aiyaṅkār appaṇiyō?
To eat rice at the choultry, is the permission of the head brahman
necessary?
3088. cattukkaḷ ōṭu cattukkaḷ cērvār, cantaṉattōṭu karuppūram cērum.
The powerful associate with the powerful, sandal paste mixes with
camphor.
3089. catturukkaḷiyum cittamāy nēci.
Love even your enemies heartily.
3090. catturu poṟāmi taṉakkē taṇṭaṉi.
An enemy's envy is his own punishment.
3091. cantiraṉippārttu nāy kulittāṟpōla.
As a dog barked at the moon.
3092.cantiraṉ caṇṭāḷaṉ vīṭṭilum pirakācikkiṟāṉ.
The moon shines even in the house of the outcast.
3093. cantiyil aṭipaṭṭavaṉukkuc cāṭci ār?
Who is the witness of him that has been flogged in the bazaar?
3094. cantik kūṭṭam pommal āṭṭam.
A crowd of people in a market-place is like a puppet-show.
3095. cantikku vantavarkaḷ tuṇi āvārā?
Will those who frequent the same market aid as friends?
3096. canniyāci vīṭu tiṇṇiyilē.
The pial-open verandah-is the home of the ascetic.
3097. canniyāci payaṇam tiṇṇi viṭṭuk kutippatutāṉ.
The pilgrimage of the mendicant is to jump off a pial.
3098. canniyācikkup paḻiya kuṇam pōkātu.
The old temper of the ascetic will not leave him.
3099. canniyāci kōvaṇattukku iccittuc camucāram mēliṭṭatu.
As an ascetic became a householder when he coveted a cloth.
3100. canniyāciyik kaṭitta nāykkup piṉṉālē narakamām canniyācikku muṉṉālē maraṇam.
They say that the dog that bites an ascetic will go to hell eventually,
the ascetic will die before.
3101. canniyāciyi nintittavaṉukkup piṉṉālē narakamām.
It is sid that hell awaits him who reproaches an ascetic.
3102. canniyācikkum cātimāṉam pōkātu.
Even an ascetic feels proud of his caste.
3103. capikkōḻi ākātu.
Timidity before an assembly is bad.
3104. capiyilē nakkīraṉ aracilē viṟcēraṉ.
In the assembly of the learned he is a nakkiran-a sage-in a royal
council a cheran-a king skilled in archery.
3105. cappāṇikku noṇṭi caṇṭappiracaṇṭam.
A lame man is a hero before a cripple.
3106. camaṇa canniyācikkum vaṇṇāṉukkum campantam eṉṉa ?
What has a naked mendicant to do with a washerman?
3107. camayam vāyttāl namaṉiyum palakāram ceyvāṉ.
When opportunity offers he may eat even Yama as a cake.
3108. camayam vāyttāl kaḷavu ceyvāṉ.
He will steal if opportunity offer.
3109. camarttaṉukku ētum peritalla.
Nothing is too great for a clever man.
3110. camarttilē kuṇṭu pāyumā?
Do cannon balls fly with intelligent force?
3111. camartti eṉṉa peṟṟāḷ caṭṭic cōṟu tiṉṉap peṟṟāḷ.
What did the clever matron get, she got rice in chatty fulls.
3112. camarttuḷḷa cēvakaṉukkup pullum āyutamām.
Even a blade of grass is a weapon in the hands of a skilful warrior.
3113. camarttuḷḷa cēvakaṉukku vāḷ paḻutāṉālum kēṭu illi.
Though his sword is injured, the skilful soldier suffers no loss.
3114. camarttukkuc caṉiyaṉ.
In power, equal to Saturn.
3115. camikki aṟiyātavaṉ caturaṉ alla.
He who cannot comprehend a sign is not clever.
3116. camikki kāṭṭic caṇṭikku aḻikkiṟāṉ.
He is inciting to fight by showing signs.
3117. camucārakkuṭṭu veḷi iṭṭāl naṣṭam.
It is a loss for family broils to get abroad.
3118. camuttiramum cākkaṭiyum cariyā?
Are the ocean and a ditch alike?
3119. camuttirattilē ēṟṟam pōṭṭatu pōl irukkiṟatu.
Like planting a picotta on the sea-side.
3120. camuttirattiṟkum cāṇ kuṇṭukkum emmāttiram?
How vast the difference between the ocean and a span-wide ditch?
3121. camuttira vaṉkaṇaṉ caṇṭāḷaṉ.
A deep treacherous fellow is a wretch.
3122. camiyal vīṭṭilē nāy nuḻintāṟpōla.
As a dog crept into the cooking room.
3123. camiyal vīṭṭilē muyal tāṉē vantatupōla.
As a hare of its own accord came into the cook room.
3124. campattum vipattum kūṭavē irukkiṟatu.
Abundance and stritness are closely allied.
3125. campaḷam arippaṇamāṉālum caluki irukkavēṇṭum.
Though one's income is only half a fanam, an air of respectability must
be preserved.
3126. campaḷam arippaṇamāṉālum nampiṉavarik kākka vēṇṭum.
Though one's hire is but half a fanam dependents must be protected.
3127. campaṉṉa kirakastaṉ vantāṉ, cempu tavaliyi uḷḷē vi.
Our rich and honest neighbour is come, remove the copper vessels out of
sight.
3128. cayiṉaṉ kiyil akappaṭṭa cīlippēṉ pōl.
Like lice in a cloth that had been in the hands of a Jaina mendicant.
3129. carakkuk kaṇṭa iṭattilē piḷḷi peṟukiṟatu.
Bringing forth a child where the required conveniences are found.
3130. carakkuk kaṇṭa iṭattilē piḷḷikku aviḻtam koṭukka niṉikkiṟatu pōla.
Like intending to give medicine to her child where conveniences are
found.
3131. caruku arikka nēram aṉṟi kuḷir kāya nēram illi.
He has only just time to collect dry leaves; he has not time to warm
himself.
3132. carukkari eṉṟāl tittikkumō?
Is the word sugar sweet?
3133. carukkarip pantalilē tēṉmāri peytatupōl.
A honey-fall in a sugar pandal-an open shed.
3134. carukkariyum maṇalum cariyā?
Are sugar and sand alike?
3135. carukkariyum māvum cariyā?
Are sugar and flour alike ?
3136. carukkari tiṉṟu pittam pōṉāl, kippu maruntu ēṉ tiṉṉavēṇṭum.
If bile can be removed by taking sugar, why take bitter medicine.
3137. calam nuḻiyāta iṭattil eṇṇeyum, eṇṇey nuḻiyāta iṭattil pukiyum nuḻiyum.
Oil gets in where water cannot, and smoke enters where oil cannot.
3138. calliya cārattiyam ceyyātē.
Drive not the coach by magic.
3139. cavātilē mayir vāṅkiṉatu pōla.
Like pulling out hir from civet.
3140. caḷi piṭittatō caṉiyaṉ piṭittatō?
Are you suffering from catarrh, or has Saturn seized you?
3141. caḷukkaṉ taṉakkuc catturu, cavaḷikkāraṉukku mitturu.
The fop is his own enemy, but a friend of the cloth merchant.
3142. caṟcaṉar uṟavu carkkarip pāku pōla.
The friendship of the good is agreeable as molases of sugar.
3143. caṉattōṭu caṉam cērum cantaṉattōṭu karppūram cērum.
Men associate with men, camphor and sandal paste blend together.
3144. caṉap palam iruntāl maṉap palam varum.
When a man has a strong party he will act with decision.
3145. caṉi nīrāṭu.
Bathe on Saturdays.
This proverb is regarded by the people among whom I have live as
equivalent to a command, and is obeyed as such, by observing Saturday as
a bathing day.
Some interpreters put another construction upon it, making the word,
usually translated Saturday, a verb, meaning to spring as water, and
render it, bathe in springing, fresh, cool water. In some parts of the
country, men bathe on Saturday, while women bathe on Fridayor Monday. To
bathe on Tuesday is regarded as fatal if
repeated agin and agin.
3146. caṉi piṭittatō caṉiyaṉ piṭittatō?
Has Saturn seized, or one possessed by him, seized ?
3147. caṉip piṇam taṉippōkātu.
A Saturday corpse goes not alone.
To avert evil as far as possible, it is not uncommon, with reference to
the previling opinion expressed in this proverb, for Hindus to bury a
fowl when a death happens on Saturday. A Native friend informed me that
he saw a chicken put into the coffin of a deceased Native Christian. I
have been informed that on some occasions the fowl is carried alive to
the place of incremation or burial, the people scattering various kinds
of grin on the way. In this case the fowl is killed near the corpse and
its blood is sprinkled upon it.
3148. caṉiyaṉi vilikku vāṅkiṉatupōla.
Like procuring Saturn at a price.
3149. caṉiyaṉ piṭittavaḷukkuc cantiyilum kanti akappaṭātu.
A woman seized by Saturn will not find a rag even in the market.
3150. caṉiyaṉ piṭittavaḷukkuc cantiyiṟ pōṉālum puruṣaṉ akappaṭamāṭṭāṉ.
A woman seized by Saturn will not obtin a husband though she may go to
the
market.
3151. caṉṉatam kulintāl vīṟāppu eṅkē?
If oracular power has failed, whence the ground of exultation ?
3152. cākat tirikiṟāṉ caṇṭāḷaṉ cāppiṭṭut tirikiṟāṉ peṇṭāḷaṉ.
The vicious wanders about famished; the householder is in the enjoyment
of plenty.
3153. cākat tuṇintavaṉukkuc camuttiram nīccu.
The sea is but swimming depth to one that braves death.
3154. cākap poḻutaṉṟi vēkap poḻutu illi.
There is time to die, but none to be consumed by fire.
3155. cākiṟavariyil vittiyaṉ viṭāṉ, cettālum viṭāṉ pañcāṅkakkāraṉ.
A physician will not leave one till death, an astrologer will not leave
even then.
3156. cākiṟavariyil maruntu koṭukkavēṇṭum.
Medicine must be given to the very last.
3157. cākiṟatu pōl iruntu viyāti tīrukiṟatum uṇṭu.
They who were apparently dying have recovered.
3158. cākiṟavaṉukkuc camuttiram muḻaṅkāl āḻam.
The ocean is but knee-deep to him who is dying.
3159. cākiṟavaṉ caṉiyaṉukkup payappaṭuvāṉā?
Will one who is dying be afrid of Saturn ?
3160. cākiṟa varikkum cañcalamāṉāl pōkiṟatu entak kālamō?
If anxieties attend us till death, when will they be removed ?
3161. cākiṟavarikum caṅkaṭamāṉāl vāḻukiṟatu ekkālam?
If we are to be troubled till death, when shall we enjoy prosperity?
3162. cākiṟa nāy vīrattik kāṭṭiṉāṟpōla.
As a dying dog showed courage.
3163. cākiṟa nāy vīṭṭiṉ mēl ēṟiṉāṟpōla.
As a dog about to die ascended the house top.
For a dog to get on the roof of a house is regarded as an omen of some
great calamity, and therefore the dog should be destroyed.
3164. cākkaṭic cēṟu eṉṟālum cakkaḷatti eṉṟālum cari.
The mud of a ditch and a rival wife are alike.
3165. cākkaṭip puḻu eṉṟālum cakkaḷattiyi vellappōkātu.
Though despicable as a worm in a ditch, it is difficult to overcome a
rival wife.
3166. cākkaṭip puḻuviṟkup pōkkiṭam eṅkē ?
Where shall the worm that was born and bred in the ditch go?
3167. cākkum pōkkum ēṟkātu aiyaṉ muṉ.
Excuses are of no avil before God.
3168. cākkō nōkkō ammiyār vākkō?
Is it the result of excuse, planetary influence or the matron’s word ?
3169. cāṭikku mūṭi vāyttatu pōla.
As a lid fitted to a jar.
3170. cāṭcikkāraṉ kālil viḻuvatilum caṇṭikkāraṉ kālil viḻalām.
Better to fall at the feet of an opponent than at the feet of witnesses.
3171. cāṭṭu illāmaṟ cāvu illi.
No death occurs without an ostensible cuse.
3172. cāṭṭi illāp pamparam āṭṭivikka vallavaṉ.
He is able to spin a top without a string.
3173. cāṭṭi illāp pamparam āṭumā?
Will a top spin without a string?
3174. cāṇāṉ eccil karuppukkaṭṭi, carkkari vellam uḻavaṉ eccil.
Course sugar is defiled by a chanan's-tree climber-saliva, and sugar by
that of a
ploughman.
3175. cāṇik kuḻiyum camuttiramum cariyāy niṉikkalāmā ?
Can you imagine the ocean and a dung-pit to be of equal magnitude?
3176. cāṇic caṭṭiyum caruvaccaṭṭiyum cariyāmā?
Can you compare a cow-dung chatty with a brass pan?
3177. cāṇiyum cavātum cariyākumā?
Are dung and civet alike?
3178. cāṇukkuc cāṇ vittiyācam.
It differs at every span-length.
3179. cāṇō vayiṟu carīram ellām vayiṟō?
Is your stomach a span-long, or are you all stomach?
3180. cāṇ ēṟa muḻam caṟukkukiṟatu.
To advance a span, and slide back a cubit.
3181. cāṇ kaṟ kaḻuviṉāl muḻaccēṟu.
In every span of pavement I wash, there is a cubit of deep mud.
3182. cāṇ kuruvikku muḻa vālām.
It is sid that a span-long bird has a cubit-long til.
3183. cāṇ caṭikku muḻak kayiṟā?
What, a cubit of string for a span of matted hir ?
3184. cāṇ ceṭiyilē muḻat taṭi veṭṭalāmā?
Can a stick a cubit long be cut in a span-long copse?
3185. cāṇ paṟikku muḻat taṭi.
A cubit stick for a span drum.
3186. cāṇ pāmpāṉālum muḻat taṭi vēṇṭum.
Although the snake may be only a span-long, a stick a cubit long is
required to kill it.
3187. cāṇ piḷḷiyō āṇpiḷḷiyō?
Is it a span-long child or a bold man?
3188. cāti anta putti kulam anta ācāram.
Ideas suited to caste, manners suited to rank.
3189. cātimāṉamum camayamāṉamum canniyācikku uṇṭu.
Caste and religious distinctions obtin even among religious mendicants.
3190. cātu miraṇṭāṟ kāṭu iṭam koḷḷātu.
When the meek are enraged, even a forest will not hold-their wrath.
3191. cāturiyap pūṉi mīṉ irukkap puḷiyaṅkāyik koṇṭu pōyviṭṭatu.
The cunning cat left the fish, and carried off the tamarind fruit.
3192. cāturiyap pūṉi tayir irukkac caṭṭiyi nakkiṟṟu.
The artful cat left the curds, and licked the chatty.
3193. cāttāṉi kuṭumikkum canniyāci pūṇūlukkum muṭi pōṭukiṟāy.
Thou art tying the knot of Sattani's hir to the sacred thread of the
religious
mendicant.
3194. cāstiram poy eṉṟāl kirakaṇattip pār.
If the shasters are false, look at the eclipse.
3195. cāstiram pārttup peṇṇik koḷ kōttiram pārttup peṇṇik koṭu.
Take a wife after consulting the shasters and give a dughter in
marriage after ascertining the character of the family intowhich she is
going.
3196. cāstiram pārāta vīṭu camuttiram, pārtta vīṭu tarittiram.
The family that regards not the shasters has an ocean of wealth, whilst
the one that regards them is in poverty.
3197. cāstiram kaṟṟavaṉ tāṉē kācu.
He who is learned in the shasters is himself money.
3198. cāntuppeṭṭi pāmpuppeṭṭi āyiṟṟu.
The scent box has become a box of snakes.
3199. cāppiḷḷi peṟṟut tālāṭṭavā?
Can you dandle a still-born child?
3200. cāppiḷḷi peṟuvatilum tāṉ cāvatu nalam.
Better die than bear a still-born child.
3201. cāppiḷḷi peṟṟavaḷukkuc cantōṣam varumā?
Will she have joy who has borne a still-born child?
3202. cāppiḷḷi peṟṟālum maruttuviccikūli tappātu.
Though a still-born child is brought forth, there is no escape from the
midwife's fee.
3203. cāmi varam koṭuttālum pūcāri iṭam koṭukkamāṭṭāṉ.
Though God may bestow the gift, the priest will not suffer you to enjoy
it.
3204 cāmippayirum cakkiliyap peṇṇum camintāl teriyum.
Sami rice when boiled, and a girl of the shoe-maker class when matured,
appear to advantage.
3205. cāmpalik kiṇṭik kōḻi tāṉē vilaṅku iṭṭukkoṇṭatupōla.
As a hen fettered herself whilst scratching a rubbish heap.
3206.cāmpalit tiṉṟu veṇṇeyyip pūciṉatu pōla.
Like rubbing the mouth with butter after having eaten ashes.
3207. cārāyatti vārttup pūrāyattik kēḷu.
Pour in liquor and draw out the secret.
3208. cālāy vaṉintāl eṉṉa caṭṭiyāy vaṉintāl eṉṉa?
What matters it whether the potter makes a large or a small chatty?
3209. cālōṭē taṇṇīr cāyttuk kuṭittālum tāy vārkkum taṇṇīr tākam teḷiyum.
Though one may drink water out of a large pot, the water that one's
mother pours out allays one's thirst.
3210. cāvāmaṟ kaṟpatē kalvi, piṟar iṭattil ēkāmal uṇpatē ūṇ.
That is learning which teaches us to escape death, and that is food
which is obtined
without dependence on others.
3211. cāṉṟōr illāc capi kuṟavar cēri.
A community without learned men is a hamlet of mountineers.
3212. ciṅkam pacittāl tēriyip piṭikkumā?
When hungry will a lion prey on frogs?
3213. ciṅkam pacikku āṉiyiyē tēṭik kollum; atupōl, periyōr akkaṟippaṭṭāl
periya kāriyattiyē ceyvārkaḷ.
When lions are hungry they go in search of elephants to prey on, in like
manner, when the great are reduced to poverty they achieve great
things-to relieve their wants.
3214. ciṅkattukkup paṅkam illi.
A lion knows no danger.
3215. ciṭṭukkuruvimēl paṉaṅkāy vittatu pōl.
As a palmyra fruit was placed on a small bird.
3216. ciṭṭukkuruvimēl pirammāstiram toṭukkalāmā?
Do you discharge heavy arrows at small birds ?
3217. ciṇukku ellām piṇakkukku iṭam.
Hesitancy and delay lead to disagreeables.
3218. citamparattilē piṟanta piḷḷikkut tiruvempāvi kaṟṟuk koṭukka vēṇṭumā?
Is it necessary to teach venba-holy verses-to a child born and brought
up at
Chidambaram?
3219. citampara cakkarattip pōy pārppatupōla.
As a demon looks at the circle of Chidambaram.
This proverb refers most likely to magical diagrams generally.
3220. cittaṉ pōkkuc civaṉ pōkku āṇṭi pōkku atē pōkku.
The manner of Chittan is like the manner of Siva, the manner religious
mendicant is
like itself.
3221.cittiri mātattiṟ celvaṉ piṟantāl cīrum ciṟappum keṭum.
If a son is born in Chittiri-April-the state and reputation of the
family will be ruined.
3222. cittiri mātattiṟ piṟanta cīr kēṭaṉum illi, aippaci mātattiṟ piṟanta
atiṣṭavāṉum illi.
None born in Chittiri-April-is unfortunate, none born in Aipasi-
October-is fortunate.
3223. citti peṟāta maruntum maruntō peṟṟup paṭiyāta piḷḷiyum piḷḷiyō?
Is that medicine which does not take effect, is that which a woman has
not brought forth and reared, her child?
3224. cittirāṅki pommā ciṉṉa veṅkiṭṭammā.
Little Vengadamma is a hypocritical lady.
3225. cinēkam ceyyumuṉ ārāytal cey, ceytapiṉ aiyappaṭātē.
Form friendships after due deliberation, having done so do not giveplace
to doubt.
3226. cinēkam ceytapiṉ cōti teḷintapiṉ nampu.
On forming friendship try it, and on being convinced of its sincerity,
rely on it.
3227. cinta aṟuntu pōkiṟa mūkku enta maṭṭum irukkum?
How long will the nose last that breaks off on blowing ?
3228. cippiyilē viḻunta maḻittuḷi muttākum; atupōla, nallavarkkuc ceyta utavi
nili niṟkum.
A rin drop that falls on an oyster-shell will become a pearl, so a
benefit conferred on the virtuous will endure.
3229. cimpilē vaḷiyātatu taṭittāl vaḷiyappōkiṟatā?
If when it is a twig it cannot be bent, will it bend when it has become
a large tree?
3230. cirittāl moṭṭi vittāṟ kuṭumi.
If shaven-bald, if kept, kudumi.
3231. cirittāl kūli cēvittāṟ campaḷam.
If you shave, hire, if you serve, wages.
3232. cilvāṉak kaḷḷi celavu aṟivāḷā?
Is a woman who pilfers aware what expense means?
3233. civapūci vēḷiyil karaṭi pukuntatu pōl.
As a bear entered at the time of Siva puja.
3234. civapūci vēḷiyilē karaṭiyi viṭṭu āṭṭukiṟatā?
Is a dancing bear produced at the time of Siva puja?
3235. civaliṅkattiṉ mēl eli.
A rat, on Siva linga.
3236. civiyāṉukku aṭimippaṭṭāl kāvavum vēṇṭum cumakkavum vēṇṭum.
If subject to a palanquin bearer, one must bear both palanquins and
burdens.
3237. ciṟakilum mellicāyp poṉ aṭippāṉ.
He will beat out gold even thinner than a feather.
3238. ciṟaku illāp paṟavipōla.
Like a bird without wings.
3239. ciṟaku paṟikoṭutta paṟavipōla.
Like a bird deprived of its wings.
3240. ciṟiyārōṭu iṇaṅkātē cēmpukkup puḷi viṭṭu maciyātē.
Do not associate with the mean, do not macerate chambu greens with acid.
3241. ciṟiyārkku iṉiyatik kāṭṭātē cēmpukkup puḷi viṭṭu ākkātē.
Do not offer sweets to children, nor mix acid with chambu greens.
3242. ciṟiyōr ceyta ciṟu piḻi ellām periyōr āṉavar poṟuppatu kaṭaṉē.
It is the duty of the great to forgive the faults of inferiors.
3243. ciṟiyōr ellām ciṟiyar alla.
All that are little are not inferiors.
3244. ciṟukac ciṟukat tiṉṟāl maliyiyum tiṉṉalām.
If you eat little by little, you may consume a mountin.
3245. ciṟukak kaṭṭip peruka vāḻu.
Build a small house, and live thriftily.
3246. ciṟuka vitittavaṉ ciṟuka aṟuppāṉ.
He that sows little will reap little.
3247. ciṟu kuḻikaḷ koñcam taṇṇīrāl nirampum.
But little water is required to fill a small hole.
3248. ciṟu kuḻanti illāta vīṭum vīṭu alla, cīrakam iṭṭu ākkāta kaṟiyum kaṟi alla.
A house without an infant is not a house, nor is a curry without
seerakam-cummin-a real curry.
3249. ciṟukki ciṉṉap paṇam, ciṟukki koṇṭi mūṉṟu paṇam.
The young lady is worth a small fanam, and she requires three fanams to
adorn her
tresses.
3250. ciṟu turumpum palluk kutta utavum.
Even a small fibre may serve as a tooth-pick.
3251. ciṟuttirukkiyil vaḷiyātatu paruttirukkiyil vaḷiyumā?
Will that which did not bend when small, do so when it becomes large?
3252. ciṟu piḷḷi ceyta vēḷāṇmi viḷintālum vīṭu vantu cērātu.
The harvest of little children will never be housed.
3253. ciṟu peṇ kaṭṭiṉa ciṟṟāṭiyā?
Is it a small cloth worn by a little girl?
3254. ciṟupōtu paṭiyāta kalvi aḻukkuc cēliyil cāyam ēṟṟiṉatu pōl.
Learning not acquired in early life is like a cloth dyed when dirty.
3255. ciṟu mīṉ ellām peru mīṉukku iri.
Little fish are the prey of great fish.
3256. ciṟumiyum perumiyum tāṉ tara varum.
The lower and higher stations in society, are the result of each one's
exertions.
3257. ciṟu rūpatti uṭiya pērum arumporuḷic ceyvār: atupōla, ciṟu viti ākiya
ālamaram peru niḻalik koṭukkum.
Those who are of inferior stature may accomplish difficult things: the
seed of the banyan is small, but the tree affords a large shade.
3258. ciṟu vayatil kalvi ciliyil eḻuttu.
Learning acquired in youth, is an inscription on stone.
3259. ciṟiccālikku aṟi illi , tēvaṭiyāḷukku muṟi illi.
A prison has no apartments, a temple girl observes no relationships.
3260. ciṟṟāḷ eṭṭu āḷukkuc cari.
A young hireling is equal to eight grown up servants.
3261. ciṟṟātti piḷḷiyum piḷḷiyō cettiyiṟ palliyum palliyō ?
Is the child of a maternal aunt a child? Is a lizard on the rubbish
heapa lizard?
This refers to a species whose chirp is not regarded in augury.
3262. ciṟṟiṉpam eṇṇār maṟṟiṉpam kaṇṭavar.
Those who have tasted real happiness, will not regard inferior
pleasures.
3263. ciṟṟuṇarvōr eṉṟum ciluciluppar.
Persons of little learning are always talkative.
3264. ciṟṟūrilē pārakkūttā?
Will an extended comedy be performed in a mere hamlet?
3265. ciṟṟūṇ iṉitu.
Moderate refection is sweet.
3266. ciṟṟeṟumpic ciṟṟeṟumpum kaṭṭeṟumpik kaṭṭeṟumpum tēṭum.
Little antsseek small ants; bigants seek great ones.
3267. ciṉattāl aṟutta mūkkuc cirittāl varumā?
Will the nosecut off in anger, be restored by lughing?
3268. ciṉattālum cīr aḻiyap pēcātē.
Do not speak reproachfully though provoked.
3269. ciṉṉa ciṉṉap pēccuc ciṅkārappēccu vaṉṉavaṉṉap pēccu metta vaḻakkamāṉa pēccu.
Short expressions, beautiful expressions; florid expressions,
colloqual(?) expressions.
3270. cī eṉṟa vīṭṭilē pēyum nuḻiyātu.
Even a demon will not enter a house that has pooh-poohed him.
3271. cī eṉṟa kāṭṭilē cennāy cērumā?
Will a dog enter a jungle that is looked down upon?
3272. cīkkira putti pala īṉam.
A hasty mind is feeble.
3273. cīṭṭāḷukku oru muṭṭāḷ cerupput tūkkikku oru aṭappakkāraṉā?
Does a letter-carrier, require a helper, or a shoeboy employ one to
carry his betel pouch?
3274. cīti piṟakkavum ilaṅki aḻiyavum.
The birth of Sita was the ruin of Lanka.
3275. cīpurattup paḷḷi cettum keṭṭāṉ iruntum keṭṭāṉ.
A Palli of Sipuram is ruined when dead, as well as when alive.
3276. cīppu eṭuttu oḷittāl kaliyāṇam niṟkumā?
If the comb be concealed, will the marriage ceremony be delayed?
3277. cīraṅkattuk kākamāṉālum kōvintam pāṭumā?
Though hatched at Shrirangam, will a crow sing the prises ofGovinda?
3278. cīraṅkattukkup pōkiṟavaṉ, vaḻiyilē pāriyip paṟikoṭuttatupōla.
As a man lost his wife on his way to Shrirangam.
3279. cīraṅkattil ulakki koṭuttatu pōla.
As a pestle was given at Shrirangam.
3280. cīraṅkattukkup pōkiṟavaṉ ōriyi mārāppup pōṭṭatu pōla.
As ons going to the shrine ofShrirangamconcealed an old jackal
inhisbosom.
3281. cīr aṟṟār kiyiṟ cempoṉ vili peṟā.
Fine gold in the hands of the unthrifty is of no value.
3282. cīriyar keṭṭālum cīriyarē.
Though reduced to poverty, the virtuous are still virtuous.
3283. cīriyarkku aṉpu cey.
Be kind to the virtuous.
3284. cīlippāy īḻam pōyc cīṉi carukkari kaṭṭumā?
Will a ragged cloth go to Ceylon to tie up sugar ?
3285. cīvaṉam ceyya nāviṉi viṟkēl.
Do not make merchandise of your tongue for a livelihood.
3286. cīvaṉ pōṉāl kīrttiyum pōmā?
Will fame go when life goes ?
3287. cīṉi eṉṟu eḻuti nakkiṉāl iṉikkumā?
If you write the word sugar and lick it, will it taste sweet?
3288. cuka tukkam cuḻal cakkaram.
Grief and joy are a revolving wheel.
3289. cukattukkup piṉ tukkam tukkattukkup piṉ cukam.
After joy grief, after grief joy.
3290. cukattiyāvatu peṟavēṇṭum tavattiyāvatu peṟavēṇṭum.
We must either enjoy happiness or practise austerities.
3291. cukattip peṟṟatum alla tavattip peṟṟatum alla.
He has neither obtined happiness nor the fruit of austerities.
3292. cukam vantāl cantōṣappaṭṭut tuṉpam vantāl piṉvāṅkuvāṉēṉ?
If when prosperous you rejoiced, why draw back when adversity
supervenes?
3293. cukavāci uṭampu kaḻutip piṟappu.
A man of luxurious habits is an ass.
3294. cukkira utayattil tāli kaṭṭi, cūriya utayattiṟkuḷ aṟuttāḷ.
She who was married when Venus rose, was denuded of her marriage symbol
at
sunrise.
3295. cukkirīva ākkiṉiyāy irukkiṟatu.
It is the infliction of Sugriva.
Sugriva,a monkey, the ally of Ramachandra.
3296. cukku aṟiyāta kaṣāyam uṇṭā ?
Is there any decoction without dried ginger ?
3297. cukkut tiṉṟu mukkip peṟṟāl teriyum piḷḷi arumi.
The preciousness of children is known to her who has eaten dried ginger
and borne one.
It is common when a woman is confined, to give her a preparation of
dried ginger and other spices.
3298. cukkuk kaṇṭa iṭattilē mukkip piḷḷi peṟuvāḷā?
Will she bring forth as soon as she sees the dried ginger ?
3299. cukkuk kaṇṭa iṭattil piḷḷi peṟṟuc cūriya nārāyaṇaṉ eṉṟu peyar iṭuvāḷ.
As soon as she sees the dried ginger, she will bring forth a child, and
call it
Suryanarayana.
The name being the expression of her joy.
3300. cukkum pākkum veṭṭit tāṟēṉ cuḷcuḷ eṉṟu veyileṟi.
Shine out brilliantly, I will give you dried ginger and arica-nut.
3301. cuṅkam māṟiṉāl cuṇṇāmpum kiṭiyātu.
If taxes are heavy, even chunambu cannot be had.
3302. cuṅkamum kūḻum irukkat taṭikkum.
Taxes and gruel become heavier by being kept.
3303. cuṭar viḷakkāyiṉum tūṇṭu kōl oṉṟu vēṇum.
Though it may be a bright burning lamp, a splinter is required for
rising the wick.
3304. cuṭali ñāṉam tirumpi varumaṭṭum.
The solemn thoughts occasioned by the funeral pyre, last till each one
returns home.
3305. cuṭukāṭu pōṉa piṇam tirumpātu.
The corpse that has gone to the place of incremation will not return
3306. cuṭukāṭṭup pukiyip pārkkum kompēṟi mūkkaṉ.
The tree snake looks anxiously for the smoke of the funeral pyre.
It is sid that snakes enjoy the odour of a burning body.
3307. cuṭu keṇṭikkāka ēriyi uṭikkalāmā?
May one burst the bund of a tank in order to get fried fish?
3308. cuṭṭa kariyi nāy puraṭṭukiṟāppōla.
As a dog rolls burning charcoal.
3309. cuṭṭa caṭṭi aṟiyumā appattiṉ cuviyi?
Does the baking pan appreciate the flavour of a cake?
3310. cuṭṭa caṭṭiyum caṭṭuvamum kaṟic cuvi aṟiyumā?
Do the chatty and ladle know the flavour of curry?
3311. cuṭṭa maṇṇum pacci maṇṇum oṭṭumā?
Will burnt and moist earth unite ?
3312. cuṇṭikkāy kāṟ paṇam cumikūli mukkāṟ paṇam.
The price of the chundaki is a quarter of a fanam, its carriage three
quarters of a fanam.
3313. cuṇṭikkāy aḷavilē, cāppiṭukiṟatu pātiyā, vikkiṟatu pātiyā?
Of the food about the size of a chundikki, am I to eat half and leave
half?
3314. cuṇṇāmpil irukkiṟatu cūkṣam.
The speciality or charm ofarica-nut and betel-is in the chunambu.
3315. cutta vilaṅkōṭu cutta vilaṅkutāṉ cērum.
A clean beast will join a clean beast.
3316. cutta vīraṉukku uyir turumpu.
To the real hero life is a mere straw.
3317. cumitāṅki cuṅkam iruppatu illi.
No toll at a resting block.
3318. cummā aṟukkiṟāy eṉṉaṭā cottik kaḷākkāyi?
Why waste your time in cutting the rotten kalakki ?
3319. cummā kiṭakkiṟa tāriyi ūtik keṭuttāṉ ammāṉ.
By blowing the unused trumpet my uncle spoiled it.
3320. cummā kalam cumakkamāṭṭāṉ naṉintu mukkalam cumappāṉ.
In fair weather he will not carry one kalam of grin, in foul weather,
when drenched with rin, he carries three.
3321. cummā pōkiṟavaṉip piṭippāṉēṉ, irāttiri ellām kiṭantu pitaṟṟuvāṉ ēṉ ?
Why seize one going along, and lie groaning all the night?
3322. cummā iruntāl cōṟu āmā vāṭā cittā kāl āṭṭa.
Will boiled rice come of itself? Come along Chitta, let us shake our
legs.
3323. cummā irunta ammiyārukku arip paṇattut tāli pōtātā ?
Is not a marriage symbol worth half a fanam, a gin to the matron who is
worth nothing?
3324. cummā kiṭikkumā cōṇācalaṉ pātam ?
Can one approach the feet of Chonachalan without effort ?
3325. cuya kāriya turantaraṉ cuvāmi kāriyam vaḻavaḻa.
He who is intent about his own affairs will not mind the things of God.
3326. cuya kāriya turantaraṉ parakāriya parāmukaṉ.
Intent about his own affairs, he turns from others.
3327. curikuḻal maṉatu cari naṭappatu ār?
Who can act so as to please a woman ?
3328. curuṭṭi cōṟu iṭum pampi pāl vārkkum.
Curly hir will give rice, dishevelled hir will pour out milk.
3329. curuti cukāṉupōkam iraṇṭum ottāl mutti.
When religious precepts and physical enjoyment are in harmony, happiness
is the
resultant.
3330. curuti kuru cukāṉupavam mūṉṟum ottatu meypporuḷām.
When the vedas, the priest and enjoyment meet together, there is real
wealth.
3331. curip pūvukkum paṟip pāṭṭukkum maṇam illi.
The flower of a bottle-gourd is not fragrant, the song of a pariah is
not chaste.
3332. cuvarukkum kātukaḷ uṇṭu.
Even a wall may have ears.
3333. cuvari vittukkoṇṭu allavō cittiram eḻuta vēṇṭum ?
You must first build the wall, must you not, and then adorn it with
figures ?
3334. cuvarkkattilē tōṭṭiyum cari toṇṭimāṉum cari.
In the paradise of Indra a scavenger and a Vellala of the Tondi country
are equal.
3335. cuvarkkattukkup pōkiṟa pōtu kakkattilē mūṭṭi āmā ?
When on your way to heaven, do you carry a bundle under your arm?
3336. cuvarkkattilē pōkiṟapoḻutu kakkattilē rāṭṭiṉamā?
When on your way to heaven, do you carry a spinning-wheel under your
arm?
3337. cuvarkkattiṟkup pōkiṟa pōtu pakkattilē oru kūttiyārā?
Is one expected to take his concubine along with him to the paradise of
Indra ?
3338. cuvāmi varam koṭuttālum muṉṉaṭiyāṉ varam koṭāṉ.
Though God may grant a boon, the devotee in his presence will not.
3339. cuḷḷāppu ellām pollāppu.
Every bitter sneer leads to evil.
3340. cuḷḷic cuṭṭukkoṇṭu kaḷḷik kuṭikkiṟatu.
To grill dried fish and drink toddy.
3341. cuṟṟat tuṇiyum illi nakkat taviṭum illi.
No cloth to wear, no bran to lick.
3342. cuṟṟattiṟku aḻaku cūḻa iruttal.
It is the beauty of friendship to be surrounded-by friends.
3343. cuṟṟattamarip paṟṟi iru.
Live close by your relations.
3344. cūṭu kaṇṭa pūṉi aṭuppaṭiyiṟ cellātu.
A burnt cat shuns the fire-place.
3345. cūṭu mitikkiṟa māṭṭiṉ vāyik kaṭṭalāmā?
Is it proper to tie the mouth of the ox that treads out the corn?
3346. cūṭcattilē irukkiṟatu mōṭcam.
Important ends are attined by comparatively insignificant means.
3347. cūṭcāti cūṭcat tullipaṉ.
The great contriver who is inscrutable.
3348. cūtapalattic cukamukattiṉāl aṟi.
Know the efficacy of mercury by the glow of health on the cheeks.
3349.cūtaṉukku nīti illi.
The cheat is void of justice.
3350. cūtaṉ kolliyilētāṉ māṭu mēyum.
The ox will graze in the field of the intriguing.
3351. cūtāṉattukku aḻivu illi.
Circumspection leads not to ruin.
3352. cūtiṉāl velvatu eḷitu.
It is easy to overcome an enemy by intrigue.
3353. cūtum vātum vētaṉi ceyyum.
Gambling and boasting end in sorrow.
3354. cūtu virumpēl.
Desire not gambling.
3355. cūttiravētaṉ cāstiram pārppāṉ.
A stringed brahman observes the shasters.
3336. cūttirap pāvipōla naṭikkiṟāṉ.
He dances like a puppet.
3357. cūriyaṉkīḻē tōṉṟiṉatu ellām māyi.
All under the sun is vanity.
3358. cūriyaṉik kiyāl maṟittatupōla.
Like hiding the sun with the hand.
3359. cūriyaṉik kaṇṭa iruḷpōl.
Like darkness that has seen the sun.
3360. cūriyaṉik kaṇṭa paṉipōlē nīṅkum.
It will vanish as the dew before the sun.
3361. cūriyaṉukku muṉ miṉṉāmpūccipōl.
Like a fire-fly before the sun.
3362. cūriyaṉik kaṇṭu ulakam viḷaṅkum.
The earth is illumined by the sun.
3363. cūriyaṉik kirakaṇam piṭittatu pōl uṉṉic caṉi piṭittatu.
Saturn has seized thee as the eclipse seizes the sun.
3364. cūriyaṉ eḻumuṉ kāriyam āṭu.
Form your plans before sunrise.
3365. cūriyaṉuṭiya pirakācattukku muṉṉē miṉmiṉi viḷaṅka māṭṭātu.
Fire-flies do not shine in the presence of the sun.
3366. cūriyaṉip pārttu nāy kulittatu pōla.
As the dog barked at the sun.
3367. cūriyaṉik kallāl aṭittatu pōla.
Like striking the sun with a stone.
3368. cūlāku mīṉkaḷ oṉṟi oṉṟu mōntu koḷḷum.
Like chula fish kissing each other.
3369. cūli cūli eṉṟu cōṟṟit tiṉṟu malaṭivāyil maṇṇip pōṭukiṟatā?
Do you eat up the rice yourself on the plea of pregnancy, and put earth
into the
mouth of the barren ?
3370. cūḻa ōṭiyum vāyilālē.
Though you run round, you will have to enter by the gate.
3371. cekku ulakkiyi viḻuṅkiṉavaṉukkuc cukkuk kaṣāyam maruntāmā?
Is a decoction of dried ginger a specific for him who has swallowed the
pestle of an
oil press ?
3372. ceṅkōlukku muṉṉē caṅkētamā?
Willconspiracy aginst a just administration avil?
3373. ceṅkōl ōṅkuvaṉ tirittuva tēvaṉ.
He who holds the sceptre is the triune God.
3374. ceṅkōl kōṇa eṅkum kōṇum.
If the sceptre turn aside, its effect will be felt every where.
3375. ceṭiyilē vaṇaṅkāttā marattilē vaṇaṅkum ?
If it would not bend as a sapling, will it bend as a tree?
3376. ceṭṭikkum māṭṭikkum ceṉmap paki.
The merchant and the farmer cherish innate hatred towards each other.
3377. ceṭṭikku vēḷāṇmi ceṉmap paki.
The merchant has a natural aversion to agriculture.
3378. ceṭṭi kūṭik keṭṭāṉ cēṇiyaṉ pirintu keṭṭāṉ.
The Chetty was ruined by taking a partner, the weaver by separating from
one.
3379. ceṭṭi pōṉa iṭam ellām vaṭṭam kāṟ paṇam.
Wherever the merchant goes there is a discount of a quarter fanam.
3380. ceṭṭi paṇattik kuṟittāṉ, cēṇiyaṉ kālik kuṟittāṉ.
The merchant reduced the money, the weaver lessened the width of the
cloth.
3381. ceṭṭi paṭi vellumā, cēṟṟut tavaḷi kaṭikkumā?
Can merchants disperse an army, can a toad inflict a wound?
3382. ceṭṭi ciṅkārikkiṟatukkuḷḷē paṭṭaṇam paṟipōkiṟatu
While the Chetty is adorning himself, the city is besieged.
3383. ceṭṭiyārē ceṭṭiyārē eṉṟāl, cīrakam paṇa eṭi mukkāṟ paṇam eṉkiṟār.
When I respectfully call him Chettiyar, he says that a fanam weight of
cumin is sold for three fourths of a fanam.
3384. ceṭṭi vīṭṭiṟ paṇam irukkiṟatu, ālamarattil pēy irukkiṟatu.
Wealth is in the houses of merchants, demons are in banyan trees.
3385. ceṭṭikaḷ māṭu mali ēṟi mēyumā?
Will the oxen of Chetties ascend and graze on the mountins?
3386. ceṭṭiyārē vārum cantiyi oppukkoḷḷum.
Chettiyar, come and take charge of the bazaar.
3387. ceṭṭiyi nīli toṭarntatupōla.
As Nili-a cruel woman-followed the Chetty.
3388. ceṭṭikku iṟuttup pikkum iṟuttēṉ.
I pid the Chetty and also his bag.
3389. ceṭṭukku oru taṭṭu, cēvakaṉukku oru veṭṭu.
A slap for the merchant, a sword cut for the warrior.
3390. cetta nāy tirumpak kaṭiyātu.
The dead dog will bite no more.
3391. cettavaṉ vīṭṭiṟ keṭṭavaṉ ār?
Who was ever ruined in a house whose owner was dead?
3392. cettavaṉ vāyilē maṇ, iruntavaṉ vāyilē cōṟu.
Earth in the mouth of the dead, and rice in the mouth of the living.
3393. cetta māṭu pullut tiṉṉumā?
Can a dead cow eat grass ?
3394. cettavaṉ kaṇ centāmarik kaṇ, iruntavaṉ kaṇ noḷḷik kaṇ.
The eye of the dead is a red lotus, that of the living a sightless orb.
3395. cettapiṉ eppaṭip pōṉālum eṉṉa ?
No matter what becomes of one after his death?
3396. cettavaṉ tali kiḻakkē iruntāl eṉṉa mēṟkē iruntāl eṉṉa ?
What matters it whether the head of a corpse be towards the east, or
west ?
Most Hindus, and some Native Christians even, are very particularabout
the position in which they lie down to sleep. The head shouldbe towards
the south. The north is the region of Yama. The west is avoided becuse
the person so lying down may not rise agin.
3397. cetta piṇattil kaṭi uṟṟārkku utavātavaṉ.
He that does not help his friends or relations is worse than a corpse.
3398. cetta āṭu kāṟpaṇam cumikūli mukkāṟ paṇam.
The dead sheep is worth a quarter of a fanam, and three fourths of a
fanam are required to remove it.
3399. cetta piṇattic cuṟṟit tirintāṟpōla.
As they walk round a corpse.
This may refer to the custom of a relative going round a corpse three
times at the place of incremation.
3400. cettavaṉ peṇcātiyi iruntavaṉ kikkoṇṭāṟ pōla.
As the surviver took the wife of the deceased.
3401. cetta pāmpi āṭṭukiṟāṉ.
He is charming a dead snake.
3402. cettavaṉ kiyilē veṟṟili pākkuk koṭutta campantam.
Like putting betel and arica-nut into the hand of the dead.
The giving of betel and nut is one of the marriage ceremonies.
3403. cetta māṭṭi aṟukkāta katti cottik kattarikkāyi aṟukkum.
A knife that will not cut a dead cow, will cuta blighted brinjal.
3404. cetta pāmpu cuṟṟa varukiṟatē atti nāṉ māṭṭēṉ eṉṟāṟpōla.
As if one should say, aunt, I will not, the dead snake comes to coil
round my leg.
3405. cetta piṇattukku arukē iṉic cākum piṇam aḻukiṟatu.
Those who will hereafter die weep by the body of the deceased.
3406. cetta piṇattukkuk kaṇ ēṉ, civaciva āṇṭikkup peṇ ēṉ ?
Why an eye to the corpse, why a wife to the Saiva mendicant?
3407. cettāl teriyum ceṭṭiyār vāḻvu.
The wealth of the Chetty will be known after death.
3408. ceppaṭi vitti eppaṭic ceykiṟāṉ?
How dexterously he juggles?
3409. ceppillāk kuṭikku appāp paṭṭamā?
Is the title of Appa suited to a family that possesses no brass
utensils?
3410. ceppum pantum pōla.
Like a box and a ball.
3411. cemparampākkattāṉ peyar peṟṟāṉ māṅkāṭṭāṉ nīr peṟṟāṉ.
Men at Champerempakum get a name, those at Mangadoo get water.
3412. cempāl aṭitta kācum koṭāṉ.
He will not give even a copper coin.
3413. cempālikkuk karumpu āli valitu.
Black wood is stronger than red.
3414. cempu naṭamāṭak kuyavaṉ kuṭipōkiṟāṉ.
Copper utensils being introduced, the potter removes.
3415. cemmaṟi āṭu veḷiyē ōṭat tiruṭṭu ōṉāy uḷḷē.
The sheep iswithout, the thievish wolf is within.
3416. ceyamuḷḷa maṭṭum payam illi.
While successful he knows no fear.
3417. ceyta viṉi ceytavarkku eytiṭum.
Every man's actions will cleave to him.
3418. ceytārkkuc ceyvatu cettapiṟakō ?
Is it after death that benefits are to be requited?
3419. ceyyum toḻil ellām cīrtūkkip pārkkil neyyumtoḻilukku nikar illi.
When all occupations are duly weighed, that of weaving will appear
unequalled.
3420. ceyviṉi tiruntac cey.
Do well, what you have to do.
3421. ceruppāl aṭittuk kutirik koṭi koṭuttāṟpōla.
Like giving a horse in compensation, to one who has been slippered.
3422. ceruppukku accāram turumpu.
The earnest pid for shoes is a straw.
3423. ceruppiṉ arumi veyilil teriyum neruppiṉ arumi kuḷiril teriyum.
The value of shoes is appreciated when the sun is hot, the value of fire
is known
when the weather is cold.
3424. ceruppukkākak kālit taṟikkiṟatā?
Is the foot to be cut off to try on the shoe?
3425. ceruppuk koḷḷukiṟatu kālukkō talikkō?
Is it for the feet or head, that one buys shoes?
3426. celaviṟ kuṟinta varavāṉāl cēmappaṭukiṟatu eppaṭi?
If expenses exceed income how can one thrive ?
3427. celavu atikam varavu pōtātu.
Expenses exceed the income.
3428. ceyyum eṉaccē eṉakku iraṇṭu eṉkiṟatu.
On my saying do it, he replies give me two.
3429. celavu illāta ciṅkāram pōla.
Like ornamentation that costs nothing.
3430. celavōṭu celavu kantap poṭikkuk kāṟ paṇam.
A quarter fanam for sweet scented powder, over and above the expenses
already incurred.
3431. cellam collukku añcumā?
Will a spoiled child fear rebuke?
3432. cellam caṟukkutā vācaṟpaṭi vaḻukkutā?
Does indulgence cuse you to slip, or are the door-steps slippery?
3433. cellap piḷḷi cīli uṭātām piḷḷi peṟumaṭṭum.
A spoiled child will not pot on clothes till it becomes a mother.
3434. cellāta kācu eṉṟikkum cellātu.
A coin not current is always so.
3435. cellum kācukku vaṭṭam uṇṭā ?
What discount for current cash?
3436. cellum cellāttukkuc ceṭṭiyār irukkiṟār.
There is a Chetty who can say whether the coin is current or not.
3437. celvam paramaṇṭalam cellātu, ellā maṇṭalattum cellum.
Wealth goes anywhither but to heaven.
3438. celvam uṇṭākum kālam, ceyki uṇṭu, vallami uṇṭu.
In time of prosperity there is exertion and power.
3439. celvam cerukkukiṉṟatu kācukku vaḻi illi.
He is proud of his wealth though he has not means to obtin a cash.
3440.celvapporuḷ koṭuttāl kuṟiyum, kalvip poruḷ kuṟiyumō?
On giving, wealth diminishes, will learning also so diminish ?
3441. celvam cerukkukiṉṟatu vācaṟpaṭi vaḻukkukiṉṟatu.
Wealth makes one proud, the door-steps are slippery.
3442. celvarkku aḻaku ceḻuṅkiḷi tāṅkutal.
It is an honor to the wealthy to protect their relatives.
3443. celvaṉ collukku añcāṉ, vīraṉ pōrukku añcāṉ.
The wealthy fear no orders, the hero is not afrid of war.
3444. celvam niliyil, cēṭṭaṉ kīḻ iru.
Wealth is impermanent, live in subjection to a superior.
3445. ceviṭaṉ kātil caṅku ūtiṉatupōla.
Like blowing a conch in the ear of the deaf.
3446. ceviṭaṉum kuruṭaṉum kūttup pārttāṟpōla.
As the deaf and the blin attended a comedy.
3447. ceviṭaṉ pāṭṭuk kēṭṭa campantam.
As the deaf listened to a song.
3448. ceviṭu iruntāl ūmi illiyā?
If oneis deaf is he not dumb, also ?
3449. ceṉmak kuruṭaṉukkuk kaṇ kiṭittatu pōl.
As one born blind obtined his sight.
3450. ceṉṟa kāriyattip pārttu varum kāriyatti aṟi.
Learn the future by looking at things past.
3451. ceṉṟa iṭam ellām ciṟappē kalvi.
Learning distinguishes one whithersoever he goes.
3452. cēta niṉiviṟkup pūtam cirikkum.
A demon lughs at malevolent thoughts.
3453. cēmpu koyyac ciṟṟarivāḷ ēṉ?
Why a small sickle to gather chembu greens?
3454. cēra iruntāl ceṭiyum paki, tūra iruntāl tōṭṭiyum uṟavu.
If too near even a shrub-a worthless fellow-is an enemy, if distant even
a scavenger is a friend.
3455. cēriṭam aṟintu cēr.
Associate with the agreeable.
3456. cēriyum ūrum celvamum kalviyum.
A hamlet, a country, wealth and learning.
3457. cērkki vācaṉiyā iyaṟki vācaṉiyā?
Is the habit natural or acquired?
3458. cērntavar eṉpatu kūrntu aṟintapiṉ.
Regard those as friends whose sincerity has been carefully tested.
3459. cēlimēṟ cēli kaṭṭit tēvarampi āṭiṉālum, ōlimēl eḻuttāṇi ūṉṟum peṇ
ākātu.
Though one wear cloth upon cloth, and is able to dance like a celestial,
she is not to be desired if she can press a style on a palm leaf, i. e.,
if she can write.
A strong dissuasive aginst female education. The proverb is
nevertheless in harmony with the sentiments of the majority of Hindus
who have received high education in English.
3460. cēṟṟilē putinta āṉiyik kākamum kuṭṭum.
Even a crow will peck an elephant when it is in the mud.
3461.cēṟṟilē ceṅkaḻunīr pūttāṟpōla.
As the water-lily blossoms in mud.
3462. cēṟṟilē nāṭṭiya kampam pōla.
Like a pole set up in mud.
3463. cēṟṟilē kiṭakkiṟa erumiyit tūkkuvāṉ ēṉ?
Why lift up the buffalo that wallows in the mire?
3464. cēṟṟāl eṭutta cuvar.
A wall of mud.
3465. cēṉikkup paṭṭamō cēṉāpatikkup paṭṭamō?
Are honorary distinctions bestowed on an army, or on its leader ?
3466. ciki aṟiyār caṟṟum aṟiyār.
Those who cannot take a hint know nothing.
3467. civa muttiyā mutaliyārkkuc camittuppōṭa vaḷḷuvap paṇṭāram.
The cook of the vegetarian Muttiya of the Modelly caste, is a pariah
mendicant.
3468. cokkaṉukkuc caṭṭi aḷavu.
The size of the chatty is enough for the Chetty's attendant.
3469. cokkaṭṭāṉ viḷiyāṭṭup pollāta cūtu.
The game of drughts is ruinous.
3470. coṭṭivāḷik kuṭṭi pōla.
Like the young of a chotti vali fish.
3471. corukik kiṭanta akappiyum cōṟu aḷḷap puṟappaṭṭatu.
Even the ladle that had been cast aside as useless, is agin serving out
rice.
3472. collac collap paṭṭi peṇṇip peṇṇip peṟṟāḷ.
Though agin and agin forbidden, Patti-a strumpet-brought forth only
dughters.
3473. collac colla māṭṭi maṇṇit tiṉkiṟāṉ.
Though frequently forbidden, Matti-a fool-eats earth.
3474. collāmal irukkiṟavaṉē paṇṭitaṉ.
He who is of few words is a pundit.
3475. collic ceyvār ciṟiyōr, collāmaṟ ceyvār periyōr, colliyum ceyyār
kayavar.
Inferiors may keep their word, the great do a thing without promising,
but the wicked act not even after making a promise.
3476. collik koṭutta collum kaṭṭik koṭutta cōṟum etuvariyil?
How long will the words put into one's mouth, and the rice tied up for a
journey avil us?
3477. collukiṟatu oṉṟu ceykiṟatu oṉṟu.
Saying one thing, and doing another.
3478. collukkum poruḷukkum eṭṭātāṉ cōtikkum cātikkum naṭuvāṉāṉ.
He whom neither language nor wealth can influence, has come between God
andman.
3479. collum col ākkamum kēṭum tarum.
A word uttered may bring wealth as well as ruin.
3480. collum poruḷum tōṉṟum kalvi.
Learning is conversant with words and things.
3481. col vallaṉi vellal aritu.
It is difficult to overcome the eloquent.
3482. colvaḷam illā naṟkati collil atuvē turkkati.
A good story badly told, soon loses its effect.
3483. colvatu yārkkum eḷitu, ariyavām colliya vaṇṇam ceyal.
Saying is easy, but doing is difficult.
3484. colvatu ilēcu ceyvatu allavā pirayācam?
It is easy to profess, but difficult to perform, is it not?
3485. colvār coṉṉālum kēṭpārukku mati illiyā?
No matter what others say, have not those who hear, sense to judge for
themselves?
3486. coṟintu tēyāta eṇṇeyum parintu iṭāta cōṟum pāḻ.
Oil applied without rubbing the head, and boiled rice given with
ill-will, are useless.
3487. coṟiyān tavaḷiyum vēṭṭi āṭukiṟatām.
It is sid that even toads go a hunting.
3488. coṟpēcciyum kēḷāṉ cuyaputtiyum illi.
He has no sense, he will not listen to advice.
3489. coṟṟiṟam kūṟal kaṟṟavarkku aḻaku.
It becomes the learned to explin the force of words.
3490. coṉṉatic collum kiḷippiḷḷi.
The parrot will utter what it is tught.
3491. coṉṉatic collaṭi cuṇikeṭṭa mūḷi.
Say what you are told, you senseless, deformed wretch.
3492. coṉṉatu irukkac curi piṭuṅkukiṟāy.
Neglecting what you were told to do, you pull up the beans.
3493. coṉṉapaṭi kēṭkāviṭṭāl maṇṇi veṭṭi māp paṭippēṉ.
If you do not do as I say, I will make an offering of earth.
Addressed to a demon when driving him out.
3494. coṉṉāl veṭkam aḻutāl tukkam.
It is shameful to tell it, and pinful to weep over it.
3495. coṉṉāṟ perumpiḻi cōṟeṉṟāṟ paṭṭiṉi.
As regards rice, he is famishing, but it would be a fault to say so.
3496. cōmpal illāt toḻil cōtaṉi illāt tuṇi.
Untiring service is reliable help.
3497. cōmpalukkut toṭarcci vaṟumi, cummā iruttalukkuc toṭarcci mūṭam.
Indolence leads to poverty, inaction to ignorance.
3498. cōmpalē cōṟu iṉmikkup pitā.
Indolence is the parent of want.
3499. cōmpēṟikku vāḻippaḻam tōlōṭē.
The sluggard eats his plantin, skin and all.
3500. cōḻamaṇṭalamō cūtumaṇṭalamō ?
Is it the realm of Chola, or the realm of deceit?
3501. cōḷiyip piṭuṅkik koṇṭā picci pōṭukiṟatu?
Is it after snatching away his bag one gives alms to a beggar ?
3502. cōṟu eṉṉa ceyyum coṉṉavaṇṇam ceyyum.
What can rice effect? whatever you like.
3503. cōṟu cintiṉāl poṟukkalām, cuṇi cintiṉāl poṟukkalāmā?
If rice be spilt it may be picked up; but if one loses his sense of
honour can he recover that?
3504. cōṟu cintiṉāl poṟukkalām nīr cintiṉāl poṟukkalāmā?
If rice be spilt it may be picked up, can water?
3505. cōṟṟilē kiṭakkiṟa kal eṭukkamāṭṭātavaṉ mukaṉikkal eṭuppāṉā?
Can he who would not pick a stone out of the rice, lift up the stone
lintel of a temple gateway?
3506. cōṟṟil irunta kal eṭātavaṉ cēṟṟil kiṭakkiṟa erumiyit tūkkuvāṉā?
Can he who will not pick a stone out of his rice, lift a buffalo out of
the mud ?
3507. cōṟṟukku vīṅkiṉa nāyē māṭṭup poṅkalukku vāyē,
Thou dog, greedy of boiled rice, come to the January ox-festival.
3508. cōṟṟukku illāta pūcaṇikkāy pantalilē kaṭṭi āṭṭavō ?
Of what use is a pumpkin which will not be avilable for food, is it to
be suspended to a pandal swing?
3509. cōṟṟuc cuviyōṭu totti vanta noḷḷi.
The blind that came drawn by the smell of rice.
3510. cōṟṟukkuk kēṭum pūmikkup pāramumāy irukkiṟāṉ.
He is a waste of rice, he is a burden to the earth.
3511. cōṟṟik koṭuttuk kaḻutti aṟukkiṟatā ?
What, to cut one's throat after giving rice ?
3512. ceḷariyam pēcēl.
Boast not of your strength.
3513. ñāpakam illi eṉṟu evaṉum colvāṉ, ñāṉam illi eṉṟu evaṉum collāṉ.
Any may say I have forgotten, none says I have no sense.
3514. ñāyap piramāṇam illāta kurukkaḷ vīṇ.
Teachers without moral rules are vin.
3515. ñāṉamum kalviyum nāḻi ariciyilē.
Wisdom and learning are contined in a measure of rice.
3516. ñāṉam ellām oru mūṭṭi , ulakam ellām oru kōṭṭi.
Collective wisdom is a bundle, and the whole world a fort.
3517. ñāṉam illāc cēyarkaḷ āviṉ kaṉṟilum atikamalla.
Ignorant children, are not better than calves.
3518. ñāṉam taṉattiyum kaṉattiyum koṭukkum.
Wisdom gives wealth and honour.
3519 ñāṉikku illi iṉpamum tuṉpamum.
The wise are not affected by pleasure and pin.
3520. ñāṉikkum mūṭaṉukkum caṅkāttam illi.
A wise man and a fool do not associate.
3521. takappaṉ veṭṭiṉa kiṇaṟeṉṟu talikīḻāy viḻuvārkaḷā?
Will they fall headlong into the well becuse their father dug it ?
3522. takappaṉukku iṭṭatu taliccaṉukku.
What was given to the father, will be entiled on his first-born.
3523. takappaṉukkuk kaṭṭak kōvaṇattukku vaḻi illi, makaṉtañcāvūr maṭṭum naṭippāvāṭi
pōṭac coṉṉāṉām.
Whilst the father is without waist-cloth, his son, it is sid, asked him
to spread cloth on the ground to walk on as far as Tanjore.
3524. takappaṉik koṉṟa pāvam māmiyār vīṭṭil āṟu mācam iruntāl pōkum.
The murder of a father may be expiated by residing for six months in the
house of one's mother-in-law.
The Hindus account it disgraceful in a son-in-law to live in the house
of a mother-in-law at her expense. Bharata when enumerating the various
sins he would be guilty of, if he coveted the throne of his elder
brother Rama, mentions this as one.
3525. takappaṉ paṭṭam piḷḷikku allavā?
The title of the father descends to the son, does it not ?
3526. takappaṉ tēṭak karttaṉ, piḷḷi aḻikkak karttaṉ.
The father acquires wealth, the son destroys it.
3527. takappaṉ paṭṭattip piḷḷikkuk kaṭṭiṉāl takappaṉ cāṣṭāṅkataṇṭam ceyyavēṇṭum
allavō?
If the title of the father descend to the son, the father must prostrate
himself before him, must he not?
3528. takkōṉ eṉat tiri.
So live as to be reputed a deserving man.
3529. taṅkamuṭi cūṭṭiṉālum taṅkaḷ kuṇam viṭār kacaṭar.
Though crowned with gold, the base will not abandon their natural
dispositions.
3530. taṅkac cūrikkattiyeṉṟu aṟuttuk koḷḷalāmā?
May one cut himself with a knife becuse it is made of gold?
3531. taṅkattik kuvikkiṟēṉ eṉṟālum, taṉ putti viṭukiṟatu illi.
Though I promised to give a heap of gold, he persisted in his own ideas.
3532. taṅkattūḷ akappaṭṭālum ceṅkaṟṟūḷ akappaṭātu.
Though gold-dust is procurable, brick-dust is not.
3533. taṅkatti urukki viṭṭatu pōla.
As fine gold was melted and poured out.
3534. taṅkam tariyilē taviṭu pāṉiyilē.
Gold scattered on the ground, bran in an earthen vessel.
3535. taṅkam puṭattil vikkappaṭṭālum taṉ niṟam pōkātu.
Though gold is put into the fire to be refined, its hue is not lost.
3536. taṅkam tariyilē, oru kācu nārattaṅkāy uṟiyilē.
Gold scattered on the ground, and a cash-worth of lemons placed on the
swinging tray.
3537. taṅkam ellām taviṭṭukku māṟukiṟatu.
To exchange all his gold for bran.
3538. taṅkicci piḷḷi taṉ piḷḷi āṉāl tavattukkup pōvāṉ ēṉ?
If the child of his sister be his, why go to perform penance?
3539. taci kaṇṭu katti nāṭa vēṇṭum.
The knife should be applied where there is flesh.
3540. taccaṉ peṇcāti tariyilē, kollaṉ peṇcāti kompilē.
The wife of the carpenter is on the floor, and the wife of the smith is
on the branch of a tree.
3541. taccaṉ peṇcāti aṟuttāl eṉṉa, kollaṉ peṇcāti kūlikku aṟuttāl eṉṉa?
What if the wife of the carpenter is bereaved of her husband ?
what if the wife of the smith is deprived of her hire?
3542. taccaṉ vīṭṭilē tayirum eccaṉ vīṭṭilē cōṟum eppaṭiccērum?
Will curds be found in the house of a carpenter, or boiled rice in that
of a niggard ?
3543. taccaṉ vīṭṭil pāṟcōṟṟi nattātē veḷḷāḷar.
O! Vellala do not long for the rice and milk in a carpenter's house.
3544. taccaṉ aṭitta talivācal ellām ucciyil iṭikka ulāvit tirintēṉ.
I walked about in all the entrances where the carpenter had put up
lintels, knocking my head aginst them.
3545. tañcam eṉṟu vantavaṉi vañcikkal ākātu.
To cheat one that has come for protection is bad.
3546. taṭavip piṭikka mayir illi avaḷ peyar kūntalaḻaki.
Not a hir to be felt; yet her name is the tressed beauty.
3547. taṭavip piṭikkak ki illi avaṉ peyar ceḷariyap perumāḷ.
He has no hand to feel and seize any thing, his name is the notoriously
strong.
3548. taṭi eṭuttāl ellām vēṭṭikkārar.
All are huntsmen who take up sticks.
3549. taṭikku miñciṉa miṭā.
A water pot too strong to be broken by a stick.
3550. taṭikku añcik kuraṅku āṭiṉatupōla.
Like a monkey dancing for fear of the stick.
3551. taṭikku mikunta miṭāvāṉāl eṉṉa ceyyalām?
If the pot is too strong for the stick, what can be done?
3552. taṭṭāṉ tāyp poṉṉilum māppoṉ eṭuppāṉ.
A goldsmith will pilfer the gold-dust even of his mother.
3553. taṭṭāṉum ceṭṭiyum kaṇ caṭṭiyum pāṉiyum maṇ.
The goldsmith and the Chetty have each two eyes, the chatty and the pot
are of earth.
3554. taṭṭāṉum ceṭṭiyum pōla.
Like the goldsmith and the merchant.
3555. taṭṭāṉic cērnta taṟutali.
The fool-hardy who associated with the goldsmith.
3556. taṭṭāṉ tāḻap paṟantāl tappāmal maḻi varum.
If a butterfly flies low, it is sure to rin.
3557. taṭṭārac cittu taṟiccittu vaṇṇārac cittukku varātu.
The tricks of a goldsmith and of a weaver, are not equal to those of a
washerman.
3558. taṭṭāṉiṭattil irukkiṟatu, allatu kumpiṭucaṭṭiyil irukkiṟatu.
It is either in the possession of the goldsmith, or in his vessel.
3559. taṭṭip pēcuvār illāviṭṭāl tampi caṇṭap piracaṇṭaṉ.
When there is none to contradict tambi is very fierce.
Tambi, a younger brother, is often used in a friendly way, when
addressing a junior.
3560. taṇinta villuttāṉ tikkum.
It is the bent bow that will shoot.
3561. taṇṭilē pōṉāl iraṇṭil oṉṟu.
When you go into the army, one of two-life or death.
3562. taṇṭiṟ pōṉāl iraṭṭippuc campaḷam.
Double remuneration to those who go into the army.
3563. taṇṭu oṉṟu ili mūṉṟu āri aṭā mantiri.
The ari plant, O minister, has one stalk and three leaves.
3564. taṇṇīril irukkiṟa tavaḷi kuṭittatik kaṇṭa tār kuṭiyātatik kaṇṭattār?
Who knows whether the frog in the water drinks, or that it does not
drink?
3565. taṇṇīr vennīrāṉālum neruppi avikkum.
Although cold water be heated, it will quench fire.
3566. taṇṇīrilē viḷinta upput taṇṇīrilē kariya vēṇṭum.
Salt produced from water, must be dissolved in water.
3567. taṇṇīril amuṅkiṉa muṭṭi uppup pōṭak kiḷampum.
An egg submerged in water will float, if salt be put upon it,
3568. taṇṇīrum mūṉṟu piḻi poṟukkum.
Even water will forgive a fault three times.
Referring to a person who rises to the surface three times after having
fallen into deep water.
3569. taṇṇīr kuṭitta vayiṟum teṉṉōli iṭṭa kātum cari.
The belly that has taken in water, and the aperture in the ear that
has received a circlet of ola, are alike-each enlarges as pressed.
3570. taṇṇīriyum tāyiyum paḻikkalāmā?
May one abuse water, or one's mother?
3571. taṇṇīril iṟaṅkiṉāl tavaḷi kaṭikkum eṉkiṟāṉ.
He says that the frog will bite him, if he descends into the water.
3572. taṇṇīril irukkiṟa tavaḷiyit tariyil eṭuttu viṭṭatu pōla.
Like turning out, on dry land, a frog that lives in the water.
3573. taṇṇīril iṟantavarilum cārāyattil iṟantavar atikam.
The number drowned in alcohol, is in excess of those drowned in water.
3574. taṇṇīrum kōpamum tāḻnta iṭattilē.
Water and anger seek low-persons or places.
3575. tattuvam aṟintavaṉ tavaci.
He is an ascetic who understands the philosophy of nature.
3576. tanti col mikka mantiram illi.
There is no mantra superior to one's father's advice.
3577. tappil āṉavaṉi uppilē pōṭu.
Put the illegitimate in salt.
3578. tappi aṭittavaṉ tātaṉ, caṅku ūtiṉavaṉ āṇṭi.
A Vaishnava mendicant beats a drum, and a Saiva mendicant blowsa conch.
3579. tamiḻukku iruvar tavattukku oruvar ākum.
In learning two, in austerities one.
3580. tam iṉam tammik kākkum vēli payirik kākkum.
One's own kin will preserve one, a hedge will guard a crop.
3581. tampi kāl naṭiyilē pēccup pallakkilē.
My younger brother is on foot, and his talk is in a palanquin.
See proverb 3559.
3582. tampi pēccit taṇṇīrilē eḻutavēṇṭum.
The sayings of the young man must be written on water.
3583. tampi piṭitta muyalukku mūṉṟē kāl.
The hare my younger brother cught, has only three legs.
3584. tampi moṇṭatu camuttiram pōla.
The water my younger brother drew, is like a sea.
3585. tampi uḻuvāṉ mēḻi eṭṭātu.
My younger brother can plough, but he cannot reach the handle of the
plough.
3586. tampi piṟakkat tarimaṭṭam āyiṟṟu.
As soon as my younger brother was born, the house was levelled to the
earth.
3587. tampi camarttaṉ uppillāk kañci kalam kuṭippāṉ.
My younger brother is very clever, he can drink a kalam of kanjiwithout
salt.
3588. tampi cōṟṟukkuc cūṟāvaḷi vēlikku vārāvaḻi.
My brother is like a whirlwind, as a rice consumer, but very tardy at
work.
3589. tayirukkuc caṭṭi ātāram caṭṭikkut tayir ātāram.
The chattysupports the curds, and the curds the chatty.
3590. tayirp pāṉiyi uṭittuk kākattukku amutu iṭṭāl pōla.
Like breaking the vessel that contined the curds, and feeding the
crows.
3591. tarattara vāṅkik koḷḷukiṟāyā, taliyi muḻukippōṭṭu pōkaṭṭumā ?
Will you receive by little and little what I owe, or shall I bathe my
head and begone ?
3592. tarumattip pāpam vellātu.
Vice will not conquer virtue.
3593. tarumam tali kākkum taliyi mayir kākkum.
Charity preserves the head, and the head the hir.
3594. tarmamē talikākkum.
Charity guards the head.
3595. tali aḷavum vēṇṭām aṭi aḷavum vēṇṭām kuṟukkē aḷa aṭā paṭiyi.
The measure of the head or foot is not wanted, you fellow, measure the
cloth across.
3596. tali irukkiṟa iṭattil kaḻuttu varaṭṭum pārttuk koḷvōm.
We will attend to the matter when the neck takes the place of the head.
3597. tali irukka vāl āṭumā?
Will the til wag as long as the head exists?
3598. tali iṭiyum kāyccalum taṉakku vantāl teriyum.
When overcome by them, one learns what head-ache and fever at.
3599. tali iṭiyum caṅkaṭamum taṉakku vantapiṉ teriyum.
Head-ache and trouble are understood when actually experienced.
3600. tali eḻuttut taliyic cirittāṟ pōkumā?
Can you efface the letters of destiny, written on the skull, by shaving
the head ?
3601. tali eḻutti ariyeṉṟu colluvār atalla.
They call the writing on the head, Ari-Vishnu-it is not that.
3602. tali kīḻāy iruntu tapacu ceytālum kūṭukiṟa kālantāṉ vantu kūṭa vēṇṭum.
Though one perform severe austerities, standing on the head, a thing
cannot be attined before the destined time.
3603. talikku mēṟpaṭṭa taṇṇīr cāṇ ōṭi eṉṉa muḻam ōṭi eṉṉa ?
When the water flows over your head, what matters it whether it is a
span or a cubit?
In either case death is inevitable without help.
3604. talikkut tali periyataṉam ulikku arici illi.
One is greater than the other, there is no rice to cook.
3605. talikkut tali nāyakam.
Each aims to be the head.
3606. talikku muṭiyō kālukku muṭiyō?
Is it the head or the foot that is crowned?
3607. talikku ēṟiṉāl taṉakkut teriyum.
If it ascend to the head, he will feel it.
3608. tali coṟiyak koḷḷi tāṉē tēṭikkoṇṭāy.
You yourself have procured a firebrand to scratch your head.
3609. taliccaṉ piḷḷikkārikku iṭiccaṉ piḷḷikkāri maruttu vicci.
The mother of a second child is midwife to the mother of a first child.
3610. taliccaṉ piḷḷikkāri iṭiccaṉ piḷḷikkārikkup putti coṉṉāḷām.
It is sid that the mother of a first childgave advice to the mother of
a second child.
3611. talimāṭṭiṟkuk koḷḷi tāṉē tēṭikkoṇṭāy.
You yourself have procured a firebrand for the head of your bed.
3612. talimuṟi talimuṟiyāy moṭṭi , avaḷ peyar kūntalaḻaki.
Bald from generation to generation, and yet called the matron of
beautiful tresses.
3613. tali mēlē ampu paṟantālum niliyiṟ piriyal ākātu.
It is not good to move when arrows are flying, though overhead.
3614. tali mēlē tali irukkiṟatā ?
Does a head exist above the head?
3615. tali mēlē iṭittālum tāṉ kuṉiyāṉ.
Though his head strikes the lintel, he will not stoop.
3616. taliyālē mali piḷappāṉ.
He can split a mountin with a blow of his head.
3617. taliyil eḻuti irukkat tantirattāl vellalāmā?
When destiny is written on the skull, can you avert it by artifice?
Writing on the head is another form of expression for destiny or fate,
which is supposed to be written on the skull of every individuals born
into the world. Hence the Sanscrit proverb, Kapala, Kapale Kapala mola.
"The skull, the skull, the skull is the root of all." Kapala in Sanscrit
means skull. The mundane history of all is
determined by the actions of a former state of existence. This is one of
the most prevalent doctrines of the Hindus. A man eats the fruit of his
doings, not in our sense as the same individuated being, but as the same
responsible agent who has previously been on the stage."Actions done,
whether good or bad, must of necessity be eaten;if not, they will not,
wear away in millions of ages." Smriti.
The preordinations of Brahma, as supposed to be written on the skull,
are regulated by the actions of former births. An elaborate shaster on
this subject was published in Calcutta upwards of fortyyears ago by a
Native gentleman, a Rajah of greatlearning.
3618. taliyil varum mayakkam caruva mayakkam.
When the head is crazed, all is wrong.
3619. taliyil iṭittāl tāḻak kuṉivāṉ.
He will learn to stoop when the lintel strikes his head.
3620. taliyum taliyum poruntiṉāl maliyum vantu poṟukkum.
When one head fights with another, the mountins will come to bear them
up.
3621. taliyi naṉittu āccutu, kattiyum vittu āccutu.
The head has been already wet, the razor has been already applied.
3622. taliyōṭu pōkiṟatu talippākiyōṭu pōyiṟṟu.
That which would have gone with the head has gone with the turban.
3623. talivaṉ niṟkat taṇṭu niṟkum, talivaṉ mayaṅkac carvamum mayaṅkum.
While the general isfirm his army is so, but if the chief is perplexed
all are so.
3624. tali valikkut tali aṇiyit tāṉē māṟṟip pōṭṭāṟ pōla.
As if one changed his pillow to cure head-ache.
3625. talivaṉ coṟ kēḷ, naṉṉeṟi tavaṟēl.
Obey your superior, deviate not from the path of rectitude.
3626. tali veṭṭic camuttiramēṟ pōṭalāmā?
May the head be severed and put on the sea?
3627. tavacē aṇikalaṉ, tāḻmiyē mēṉmi.
Austerity is an ornament, humility is honourable.
3628. tavaḷi tāmarikkuc camīpamāka iruntum atiṉ tēṉi uṇṇātu. atupōla, aṟivīṉar
kiṭṭa iruntālum kaṟṟuk koḷḷārkaḷ.
The toad living near the lotus drinks not its honey, the illiterate
though they live near the learned remin ignorant.
3629. tavaḷi taṉ vāyāṟ keṭum.
The frog perishes by its own mouth.
3630. taviṭu tiṉpavaṉi amutu tiṉṉac coṉṉāṟpōla.
Like requesting one who eats bran to feed on ambrosia.
3631. taviṭu tiṉpavaṉi ekkāḷam ūtac coṉṉāṟpōl.
Like telling one who is eating bran to blow a trumpet.
3632. taviṭṭi nampip pōkac campā ariciyi nāy koṇṭu pōyiṟṟu.
When she went out in expectation of fetching bran, her samba rica was
carried off by a dog.
3633. taviṭṭukku vanta ki taṉattukkum varum.
The band that is ready to steal bran will be ready to steal money also.
3634. taḻitta marattukku niḻal uṇṭu, piḷḷi peṟṟavaḷukkup pāl uṇṭu.
A flourishing tree has shade, a woman who has recently borne a child,
has milk.
3635. taḷḷat taḷḷat tāḻppāḷi, meḷḷa meḷḷa tiṟappāṉ ēṉ ?
Why open it gently agin and agin, when I am trying to bolt that door?
3636. taṉakkup piṟanta piḷḷi taviṭṭukku aḻukiṟatām ūrār piḷḷikkuk kūṭṭuk
kaliyāṇam ceykiṟāṉām.
While his own child cries for bran, he is conducting the nuptials of his
neighbour's child.
3637. taṉakkuc cantēkam aṭippikkāraṉukku iraṭṭip paṭiyām.
When uncertin regarding his means, he promises a double allowance to
his betel-pouch bearer.
3638. taṉakku aḻaku moṭṭi piṟarkku aḻaku koṇṭi.
Touching one's own comfort a bald head, in respect to others luxuriant
hir, is preferable.
3639. taṉakku miñciṉatu tarmam.
That whichis left belongs to charity.
3640. taṉakku eṉṉaveṉṟu irukkal ākātu nāykkuc cōṟu illiyāyiṉ.
One must not be unconcerned, when there is no rice for his dog.
3641. taṉakkeṉṉac coṉṉāl nāy veṭukkeṉṉap pāyum.
If one speaks to a dog, it will jump expecting to get something.
3642. taṉakku mūkkup pōṉālum etirikkuc cakuṉappiḻi vēṇṭum.
He is so intent on producing an ominous sign aginst his enemy that he
is ready to give up his own nose. He is willing to cat his own nose to
spite an enemy: to meet one without a nose is a bad omen.
3643. taṉakku eṉṟāl piḷḷiyum kaḷi veṭṭum.
If his own crop, even a child may weed it.
3644. taṉakkut takāta kāriyattiṟ piravēcikkiṟavaṉ kuraṅku paṭṭa pāṭu paṭuvāṉ.
He who undertakes a matter not suited to him, will suffer as didthe
monkey.
3645. taṉakku eṉṟāl puḻukki kalam kaḻuvi uṇṇātu.
If for his own use a slave will not wash his plate before eating.
3646. taṉakkut takāta kāriyam ceytāl ārukkum pirāṇa cētattukku varum.
Whoever undertakes an affair unsuited to him will endanger his life.
3647. taṉakku ākāta pāṉi uṭintāl eṉṉa iruntāl eṉṉa?
What matters it whether an earthen vessel not required is broken ornot?
3648. taṉakkup piṉṉāl akam iruntu eṉṉa kaviḻntu eṉṉa ?
What matters it whether one's house stands or falls after his death?
3649. taṉakku eṉṟu orutti iruntāl talimāṭṭil iruntu aḻuvāḷ.
If he has a wife she will weep at the bed's head-at his funeral.
3650. taṉakkut taviṭu iṭikkat taḷḷātu ūrukku irumpu aṭikkat taḷḷum.
He is unable to pound rice for himself, but he can beat iron for his
neighbours.
3651. taṉakku eṉṟu aṭuppu mūṭṭit tāṉ vāḻum kālattil vayiṟum ciṟukkum matiyum
perukum.
When the time comes for one to make his own fire and prepare
his meals, then his stomach will become small and the judgment
developed.
3652. taṉi vaḻiyē pōṉavaḷit tārameṉṟu eṇṇātē.
Do not regard her as your wife who goes out unattended.
3653. taṉ icciyi aṭakkāviṭṭāl atu taṉṉiyē varuttum.
He who does not control his desires will thereby come to grief.
3654. taṉ uyirippōla maṉ uyirkku iraṅku.
Feel for others as you feel for yourself.
3655. taṉ uyirit tāṉ tiṉkiṟāṉ.
He himself consumes his own life.
3656. taṉ ūrukku aṉṉam piṟa ūrukkuk kākam.
A swan in his own village, a crow in the next.
3657. taṉ ūrc cuṭukāṭṭukkum ayal ūr āṟṟukkum añca vēṇṭum.
In his own village one should fear the place of incremation, in an
adjacent country, a river.
3658. taṉ ūrukku āṉi ayal ūrukkup pūṉi.
At home an elephant, abroad a cat.
3659. taṉ ūrukkuk kāḷi ayal ūrukkup pūṉi.
A bullock at home, a cat abroad.
3660. taṉ kāriyaturantaraṉ piṟar kāriyam vaḻavaḻaveṉṟu viṭukiṟavaṉ.
He who attends to his own affairs is indifferent about the things of
others.
3661. taṉ kāriyam eṉṟāl taṉ cīliyum patikkum.
If it is his own concern, even the folds of his garment will flutter.
3662. taṉ kāriyam pārātavaṉ catikku oru puḻu puḻuppāṉ.
He who does not attend to his own affairs, will be tormented by worms at
every pore.
3663. taṉ kāriyap puli.
In his own affairs resolute as a tiger.
3664. taṉ kīrttiyi virumpātavaḷit taḷḷiviṭu.
Abandon her who is careless of her own reputation.
3665. taṉ kuṟṟam pārppavar iṅku illi.
No one on earth sees his own faults.
3666. taṉ kuṟṟam irukkap piṟar kuṟṟam pārkkiṟatā?
Do you condemn others when guilty yourself ?
3667. taṉ kuñceṉṟu vaḷarkkumām kuyiṟ kuñcik kākam.
It is sid that the crow nourishes the young of a cuckoo, under the
impression that it is her own.
3668. taṉ cōṟu tiṉṟu taṉ puṭivi kaṭṭi vīṇ col kēṭka vitiyā?
Am I destined to be unlawfully abused while eating my own rice and
wearing my own cloth ?
3669. taṉ nilattil iruntāl muyal tantiyilum valitu.
When in his own place a hare is stronger than a tusker-an elephant.
3670. taṉ nōykkut tāṉē maruntu.
The remedy of his disease is with himself.
3671. taṉ palam kaṇṭu ampalam ēṟavēṇṭum.
Having ascertined your own ability, display it in the assembly.
3672. taṉ paṇam cellā viṭṭāl tātaṉik kaṭṭi aṭikkiṟatu.
When his own coin will not pass, he ties up and beats the Vaishnav
mendicant.
3673. taṉ pallip piṭuṅkip piṟar vāyil vikkalāmā?
Can one take out his own teeth, and put them in another's mouth?
3674. taṉ pallik kuttip piṟar mūkkil vācam kāṭṭukiṟatupōla.
Like picking the teeth and holding the tooth-pick to the nose of
another.
3675. taṉ piḷḷikkup patikkātavaḷ cakkaḷatti piḷḷikkup patippāḷā?
Can she who does not feel for her own child, feel for the child of her
rival?
3676. taṉ piḷḷiyit tāṉ aṭikkat taliyāriyic cīṭṭuk kēṭkiṟatupōla.
Like applying to the village officer for permission to punish his own
child.
3677. taṉ vāyālē tāṉ keṭṭāṉ.
His own mouth ruined him.
3678. taṉ vāyālē tāṉ keṭṭatām āmi.
It is sid that the tortoise perished by its own mouth.
3679. taṉ vāyc cītēvi muṉvāyilē.
The presence of the goddess of prosperity, will be evinced in thespeech.
3680. taṉ viṉi taṉṉic cuṭum ōṭṭappam vīṭṭic cuṭum.
His own actions will burn him, a false cake will burn the house.
3681. taṉ vīṭṭuk katavip piṭuṅki ayal vīṭṭukku vittāṟ pōla.
As if one took down his door, and placed it in the house of his
neighbour.
3682. taṉ vīṭṭu viḷakkeṉṟu muttam iṭṭāṟ cuṭātā ?
If one kiss his lamp becuse it belongs to his own house, will it
therefore not burn him?
3683. taṉ vīṭu tavira ayal vīṭṭukku mēṭṭuvari eṉkiṟāṉ.
He asserts that all houses are taxed but his own.
3684. taṉ vīṭṭuk katavip piṭuṅki ayal vīṭṭukku vittu viṭṭu viṭikiṟa maṭṭum nāy
ōṭṭiṉatupōla.
Like driving away dogs till break of day, becuse he had lent his door
to a neighbour.
3685. taṉṉālē tāṉ keṭṭāl aṇṇāviyār eṉṉa ceyvār?
If a disciple is intent on his own ruin, what can his master do ?
3686. taṉṉip pukaḻāta kammāḷaṉum illi.
There is no artificer who does not prise himself.
3687. taṉṉi aṟintavaṉ tāṉē talivaṉ.
He who has studied himself is his own master.
3688. taṉṉi aṟiyāta caṉṉatam illi.
No excitement will make one forget himself.
3689. taṉṉi aṟintavaṉ talivaṉi aṟivāṉ.
He who knows himself may know his maker.
3690. taṉṉik kākkiṟ kōpattik kākkavēṇṭum.
He who would keep himself must restrin anger.
3691. taṉṉik kolla varukiṟa pacuviyum kol.
You may kill even a cow that aims to kill you.
3692. taṉṉic cirippārit tāṉ aṟiyāṉ.
He knows not who lugh at him.
3693. taṉṉip peṟṟavaḷ koṭum pāvi, peṇṇip peṟṟavaḷ makarāci.
Despising his own mother as worthless, whilst holding his mother in-law
in high estimation.
3694. taṉṉi aṟintu piṉṉip pēcu.
Know yourself before you begin to speak of others.
3695. taṉṉic cirippatu aṟiyātām pallāvarattuk kuraṅku.
It is sid that the monkey of Palaveram does not know that he is lughed
at.
3696. taṉṉit tāṉē tāṉ paḻikkumām teṉṉamarattilē kuraṅku iruntu.
It is sid that the monkey that mocks at the top of the cocoanut tree
mocks itself.
3697. tāṅkit tāṅkip pārttāl talimēlē ēṟukiṟāṉ.
He mounts on the head by reason of continued indulgence.
3698. tācari tappu taṇṭavāḷattukkuc cari.
The tabret of the Vaishnava mendicant is like cast-iron.
3699. tāṭikkup pūc cūṭalāmā?
Can you wear a garland round your beard?
3700. tāṭi paṟṟi eriyum pōtu curuṭṭup paṟṟavikka neruppukkēṭṭatu pōl.
As one asked for fire to light his cigar when his beard was on fire.
3701. tāṭciṇiyam taṉa nācam.
Kindness leads to loss of wealth.
3702. tāṭṭōṭṭakkāraṉukkut tayirum cōṟum, vicuvācakkāraṉukku vennīrum
parukkiyum.
The deceitful feasts on rice and curds, while the faithful feeds on warm
rice and water.
3703. tāṭṭōṭṭakkārarik kūṭuvatilum taṉiyē iruppatu nalam.
It is better to be alone than to associate with the frudulent.
3704. tāṭṭōṭṭakkāraṉukkut tayirum cōṟum kūṭṭāṭṭak kāraṉukkuk kūḻum taṇṇīrum.
The frudulent enjoys curds and rice, while the honest man gets only
gruel and water.
3705.tātaṉum paṟiyaṉumpōla.
Like a Vaishnava mendicant and a pariah.
3706. tātaṉ karattil akappaṭṭa kuraṅkippōl alikiṟāṉ.
He moves about like a monkey in the hands of a juggler.
3707. tātu aṟiyātāṉ pēti vittiyaṉ.
He who does not know how to feel one's pulse is an empiric.
3708. tātum illip pirātum illi.
No cuse, no complint.
3709. tāparam illā iḷaṅkoṭipōla.
As a tender creeper without a prop.
3710. tāmatam tāḻvukku ētu.
Delay will lead to ruin.
3711. tāmari ilit taṇṇīr pōl tatumpukiṟāṉ.
He trembles like a drop of water on a lotus leaf.
3712. tāmpu aṟutal tōṇṭiyum pottal.
The cord is rotten and the water pot is fractured.
3713. tāyum takappaṉum taḷḷiviṭṭa kālattil vā eṉṟu aḻitta vaṅkāravāci.
A herb that welcomed a child cast off by its parents.
3714. tāyum piḷḷiyum āṉālum vāyum vayiṟum vēṟē.
Though they are mother and child their mouths and bellies are diverse.
3715. tāyik koṉṟavaṉukku ūrilē vāti.
The prosecutor of a matricide is in his own village.
