4455. paṭikku ācāṉ iruntāl kuṭikkuc cētam illi.
If the giver of the dily allowance be alive, no loss to the family.
4456. paṭikkiṟatu tiruvāymoḻi, iṭikkiṟatu perumāḷ kōvil
To read Thiruvaaymoli, and to break down the temple of Vishnu.
4457. paṭikkiṟa piḷḷi pākkup pōṭṭāl nākkut taṭippāy pōm.
If a school boy chew betel, his pronunciation will be spoilt.
4458. paṭittu muṭṭāḷāy irukkiṟāṉ.
He is a learned fool.
4459. paṭittavaṉukkum paṭiyātavaṉukkum, kokkukkum aṉṉattukkum vittiyācampōla.
The difference between the learned and the unlearned, is as great as
that of a crane and a swan.
4460. paṭukaḷappaṭṭa paṉṉāṭi.
A palm network driven in a battle-field.
4461. paṭukaḷattil oppāriyā?
Do they sing elegies in a battle-field?
4462. paṭukkic cukam metti aṟiyātu.
The comfort of the bed is not experienced by the mattress.
4463. paṭuvatu paṭṭum paṭṭattukku irukkavēṇṭum.
Whatever hardships he may have to endure, he must have the rank to which
he is entitled.
4464. paṭi keṭṭu ōṭukiyil nari mayir piṭuṅkukiṟatō?
When an army is routed, are grey hirs pulled out ?
4465. paṭikku oruvaṉ koṭikku oruvaṉ.
One to fight, and one to give.
4466. paṭi mikuttāl araṇ illi.
If an army be large, a fortress is not required.
4467. paṭi mukattilum aṟimukam vēṇṭum.
An acquaintance is necessary even in a battle field.
4468. paṭiyātu paṭitta marumakaḷē uṉṉip paṟiyaṉ aṟukkak kaṉāk kaṇṭēṉ.
O, my dughter-in-law, who art - entertining me sumptuously, I dreamt
that thou wast being dissected by a pariah.
4469. paṭci pacittālum eṭṭikkaṉiyit tiṉṉātu.
Though a bird is hungry, it will not eat poisonous berries.
4470. paṭṭa kālilē paṭum, keṭṭa kuṭiyē keṭum.
It will hit the foot already injured, the decayed family will be ruined.
4471. paṭṭaṇattāḷ peṟṟa kuṭṭi paṇam paṟikka valla kuṭṭi.
A young one born of a city mother is clever at thieving.
4472. paṭṭaṇattu vācalip paṭalālē mūṭukiṟatō?
Can you cover up a city gate with a hurdle?
4473. paṭṭaṇattu vācalip paṭṭālē mūṭi irukkiṟatā?
Is the gate of the city veiled with silk?
4474. paṭṭaṭi vāyttāl paṇi vāykkum.
If the anvil is good, the work will be good.
4475. paṭṭavarkku uṇṭu palaṉ.
Those who have taken pins will enjoy the gins.
4476. paṭṭavaḷukku patavi uṇṭu.
She who suffered will attin advantage.
4477. paṭṭāl aṟivāṉ caṇṭāḷaṉ, maḻi peytāl aṟivāṉ veḷḷāḷaṉ.
A rogue is wise when punished, the farmer when it rins.
4478. paṭṭiṉattu nariyip paṉaṅkāṭṭu nari ēyttatām.
It is sid that the jackal of the palmyrah grove cheated the city fox.
4479. paṭṭik kāṭṭukkuc civapput tuppaṭṭip pītāmparam.
In a common village, a red garment is regarded as a sumptuous garment.
4480. paṭṭu aṟi keṭṭu aṟi pattu eṭṭu iṟuttu aṟi.
Be wise by suffering, by poverty, and by paying ten and eight fines.
4481. paṭṭu kulintāl poṭṭu.
When silk thread is untwisted, it becomes useless.
4482. paṭṭuk kattarittatu pōlap pēca vēṇṭum.
You must speak carefully as silk is cut by the scissors.
4483. paṭṭuk kōṭṭikku vaḻi eṅkē eṉṟāl, koṭṭippākku vili nūṟṟimpatu eṉkiṟāṉ.
When he was asked which was the way to Pattucotta, he sid the price of
arica-nuts is a hundred and fifty fanams.
4484. paṭṭukku aḻuvār paṇikku aḻuvār viyakattil pākkukku aḻuta pāratattik kaṇṭatu
illi.
There are who cry for a silk cloth, and there are who cry for ornaments;
I never saw any one in the world cry for betel nut.
4485. paṭṭup puṭivi iraval koṭuttu maṇi eṭuttukkoṇṭu tirivatu pōla.
Like lending a silk cloth to another, and wandering about carrying a
seat with her.
4486. paṭṭum paṭṭāvaḷiyum peṭṭiyil irukkum kāṟ kācuk kanti ōṭi ulāvum.
The silk and the velvet are kept in a box, while a rag not worth cash
walks about the street.
4487. paṭṭikkut takka paḻam kayiṟu.
An old rope suited to the well-bucket.
4488. paṇakkaḷḷi pāyil paṭāḷ.
A niggardly woman will not lie on a mat.
4489. paṇakkāraṉ piṉṉum pattup pēr, payittiyakkāraṉ piṉṉum pattup pēr.
Ten follow after a moneyed man, and ten after a fool.
4490. paṇattukkup peyar āṭkolli.
Money is called a man-slayer.
4491. paṇattukku oru ampu koṇṭu pāḻil eykiṟatu pōla.
Like buying arrows at a fanam each, and wasting them.
4492. paṇattik koṭuttup paṇikāratti vāṅkip paṟṟikkuḷḷē iruntu tiṉkavēṇṭumō?
Is it necessary for you to bide yourself in a bush, and eat the cakes
for which you have pid?
4493. paṇam iruntāl pāṣā, paṇam illāviṭṭāl pakkiri.
If I have money, Pachcha; if not money, Pakkiri.
4494. paṇam illātavaṉ piṇam.
He who has no money is a corpse.
4495. paṇam eṉṟāl piṇamum vāy tiṟakkum.
If the word money be uttered, even a corpse will open its mouth.
4496. paṇam eṉṉa ceyyum pattu vitam ceyyum.
What can money effect? it can do ten kinds of things.
4497. paṇam eṉṉa pāṣāṇam, kuṇam oṉṟē pōtum.
Why money ? it is poison ; a good disposition is enough.
4498. paṇam kulam ākum, paci kaṟi ākum.
Money, is rank; hunger, is curry.
4499. paṇam pantiyilē kulam kuppiyilē.
Money at the festive board, and rank on the dunghill.
4500. paṇikāramō ciluciluppō ?
Is the sound that of baking cakes, or is it mere frizzle?
4501. paṇṭāram piṇṭattukku aḻukiṟāṉ, liṅkam pāl cōṟṟukku aḻukiṟatu.
The religious mendicant is crying for a mouthfull of food, the linga he
worships, for rice and milk.
4502. paṇṭi paṭṭa pāṭṭip paḻaṅkiṭukil pōṭṭuviṭṭuc campā nel kuttip poṅkal
iṭukiṟāḷ.
Having put aside her former troubles on an old cadjan, she husked chamba
rice and boiled it.
4503. paṇṇap paṇṇap pala vitam ākum,
The more he makes, the more varied the forms.
4504. paṇṇiya payiril puṇṇiyam teriyum.
A man's merit may be seen in the grin he grows.
4505. pataṟāta kāriyam citaṟātu.
A thing done without haste never fails.
4506. pataṟic ceykiṟa kāriyam citaṟik keṭṭuppōkum.
A hasty proceeding will go to ruin.
4507. patiṉāṟum peṟṟup peruvāḻvum vāḻa vēṇṭum.
May you bear sixteen children, and live very prosperously.
4508. patti koḷpavaṉ mutti uḷḷavaṉ.
The devout attin heaven.
4509. pattiyattukku muruṅkikkāy koṇṭu varac coṉṉāl pāl teḷikka akattik kīri
koṇṭu varuvāṉ.
When requested to bring murunga fruit for diet, he brings coronilla
grandiflora leaves for the milk-sprinkling ceremony. See note under
proverb 4584.
4510. patti illāp pūṉi paramaṇṭalattukku ēṟumā?
Will an ungodly cat ascend to heaven?
4511. patti illāta putti acētaṉam.
Reason without devotion is folly.
4512. patti uṇṭāṉāl mutti uṇṭām.
If there be piety, there will be final happiness.
4513. pattiyōṭē pākaṟkāy caṭṭiyōṭē tīyutu.
Pagal fruits in clusters are being charred in the chatty.
4514. pattilē viḻunta pāmpum cāvātu.
Even a snake that has fallen among ten, will not die.
4515. pattilē pacali irupatilē iṭumpu.
At ten childishness, at twenty arrogance.
4516. pattiṉiyit toṭṭatum turiyōtaṉaṉ paṭṭatum.
By touching the virtuous woman, Dropati, Duriyodhana was ruined.
4517. pattu iṟutta piṉ pārac cantēkam tīrntatu.
After I pid a fine of ten, doubt was removed.
4518. pattu ariciyum vēkavilli, pāvi pirāṇaṇum pōkavilli .
Ten grins of rice wont boil, the life of the wretch wont go.
4519. pattukku mēlē oru paṟiyaṉukkum taḷḷavēṇṭum.
After ten years of age, a girl should be affianced if even to a pariah.
4520. pattup paṇam koṭuttālum ittaṉi patippu ākātu.
Though you give ten fanams, such haste is bad.
4521. pattup pērilē patiṉorām pērāy irukkavēṇṭum.
Of the ten, you must pass as the eleventh.
4522, pattu varākaṉiṟuttōm, eṉṟālum cantēkam nivirtti āyiṟṟē.
We were fined ten pagodas, however our doubts were removed.
4523. pattu vitattilum paṟiyaṉi nampalām pārppāṉi nampakkūṭātu.
In ten ways a Pariah is trustworthy, but a brahman is not.
4524. pattu ēr vittup paṭimaramum tōṟṟēṉ ettaṉi ēr vittuk kōvaṇamum tōṟṟāy?
Possessed of ten yoke of oxen I lost the plough share ; how many yoke
did you possess before you lose your waist cloth?
4525. pantam coṉṉār paṭikkākār.
Those who regard relationships are unfit for military service.
4526. pantikku muntavēṇṭum paṭikkup pintavēṇṭum.
Be first at a feast, and last at the fight.
4527. pantiyilē vēṇṭām eṉṟāl ili pottal eṉkiṟāy.
When rejected at the feast, thou sayest that the leaf is torn.
4528. payaṇakkāraṉ payittiyakkāraṉ.
A man about to set out on a journey, is a fool.
4529. payittiyamō paṇṭāramō veṉṟāl ippōtutāṉ toṭarukiṟatu eṉṟāṉ. When I ask
him whether he is mad or a mendicant, he says, it is just following.
4530. payittiyam māṟippōccu ulakkittaṭi koṇṭuvā kōvaṇam kaṭṭa.
My madness is gone, bring me a pestle to tie round my waist.
4531. paraṇi aṭuppup pāḻ pōkātu.
A fireplace made on the second lunar day will not be deserted.
4532. parampari āṇṭiyō pañcattukku āṇṭiyō?
Is he a hereditary mendicant, or a mendicant, becuse of the famine?
4533. parikāri uṟavu teruvācal maṭṭum.
The friendship of the doctor ends at the threshold.
4534. pariyattukku añcik kuruṭṭuk kaṇṇiyik koṇṭatu pōla.
Like marrying a blind woman to avoid paying the nuptial present.
4535. parivu illāp pōcaṉattiṟ paṭṭiṉi naṉṟu, piriyam illāp peṇṭiṟ pēy naṉṟu.
Hunger is preferrable to eating food given without good feeling, a
demoness is better than an unkind wife.
4536. paruttik kaṭiyilē nāykku aluval eṉṉa ?
What can a dog have to do in a cotton bazaar?
4537. paruttikkāṭu uḻukiṟataṟku muṉṉē pommaṉukku ēḻu muḻam timmaṉukku ēḻumuḻam.
Ere the cotton fields are ploughed, Pomman asks for a cloth of seven
cubits, and Timman wants one of the same length.
4538. parutti puṭiviyāyk kāyttatu.
The cotton tree produced cloth.
4539. paruttip potikku oru neruppup poṟipōla.
As a single spark to a bale of cotton.
4540. paruppilē ney viṭṭatu pōla.
As ghee was poured on beans.
4541. parumaratti aṇṭiya palliyum cākātu.
If sheltered near a large tree even a lizard will not die.
4542. pala umi tiṉṟāl oru aviḻ taṭṭum.
If one consume plenty of husks, a grin may turn up.
4543. pala carakkukkāraṉip payittiyam piṭittatu pōla.
As the vendor of curry stuff was seized with madness.
4544. pala tīṭṭukku oru muḻukku.
One ablution for many defilements.
4545. pala tuḷi peru veḷḷam.
Many drops make great flood.
4546. paruvattē payir cey.
Cultivate in due time.
4547. parōpakāramē peritu.
Benevolence is indeed great.
4548. palattavaṉukku maruntu coṉṉāṟ piṭuṅkik koṭuttut tīra vēṇṭum.
If you prescribe medicine for the strong, you must procure the medicinal
plants yourself.
4549. palattavaṉ kikku iḷittavaṉ turumpu.
The feeble is a straw in the hands of the powerful.
4550. pala nāḷit tiruṭaṉ oru nāḷikku akappaṭuvāṉ.
An old thief will one day be cught..
4551. pala pāvam tīra oru puṇṇiyam ākilum paṇṇa vēṇṭum.
One virtuous deed at least, is required to expiate numerous sins.
4552. pala maram kaṇṭa taccaṉ oru maram veṭṭamāṭṭāṉ.
The carpenter who is acquainted with various sorts of wood, is not able
to fell a tree.
4553. pala muyaṟci ceyyiṉum pakavaṉ mēl cinti vi.
After making every effort, fix your mind on God.
4554. pala vīṭṭup picci āṟāyp perukum.
The alms of many houses may flow as a river.
4555. palaṉ illāp pala nāḷilum, aṟam cey oru nāḷ peritu.
One day spent in alms giving is greater than many spent in doing
nothing.
4556. palā uttamam, mā mattipam, pātiri atamam.
The jack tree is the best, the mango is good, the pathri tree is
indifferent.
4557. pal āṭap paci āṟum.
As the teeth move hunger is appeased.
4558. pallakku ēṟa yōkam uṇṭu uṉṉi ēṟac cīvaṉ illi.
He has a palanquin, but is not able to mount it.
4559. pallakkukku mēl mūṭi illātavaṉukkum, kālukkuc ceruppu illātavaṉukkum
vicāram oṉṟē.
He whose palanquin has no top, and he who bas no shoes for his feet are
equal.
4560. pallu viḻunta puṭiyaṉ.
A snake without fangs.
4561. pallu viḻunta puṭiyaṉukku kirutā ?
Is the rage of a fangless snake dangerous?
4562. pavucu keṭṭa pākkaṭṭikku iraṇṭu pakkamum tīvaṭṭiyām.
A torch on either side of a lump of worthless sugar.
4563. paḻaka paḻakap pālum puḷikkum.
By constant use even milk will taste sour.
4564. paḻaṅkāl tūrkkavēṇṭām putukkāl veṭṭa vēṇṭām.
Do not fill up an old channel; do not cut a new one.
4565. paḻattilē paḻam miḷakāyppaḻam.
The chief of berries is the chilli.
4566. paḻattēṅkāyilē tāṉ eṇṇey,
Old cocoanuts yield oil.
4567. paḻam naḻuvip pālil viḻuntatupōla.
Like fruit slipping and falling into milk.
4568. paḻam puṇṇāḷi pātivittiyaṉ.
He who has an old sore is half a doctor.
4569. paḻikku āṉōr cilar paḻi paṭuvōr cilar.
One hasty commits the crime, and another suffers the penalty.
4570. paḻippāṉa kalvi pāvattiṉ ūṟṟu.
Despicable learning is a fountin of sin.
4571. paḻippaṉa pakarēl.
Utter no reproach.
4572. paḻutu ceytati aṟikki iṭal pāti nivirtti.
The confession of a fault removes half its guilt.
4573. paḻutta ōliyip pārttuk kuruttu ōli cirikkiṟatām.
It is sid that the young palm leaf is lughing at the dry leaf becuse
it is falling off.
4574. paḻutta paḻam kompil irātu.
Ripe fruit does not remin on the branch.
4575. paḻiya kaṟuppaṉ kaṟuppaṉē paḻiya maṇkiṇṇi maṇkiṉṇiyē.
My name old Karuppan is still my name, and my earthen basin in still my
eating dish.
4576. paḻiya kuruṭi katavi tiṟavaṭi.
O thou blind old woman, open the door.
4577. paḻiyatu mīnta iṭam kāṇiyāṭci.
The place where one gets plenty of cold rice is the right one.
4578. paḷḷam iṟittavaṉ paṅku koṇṭu pōkiṟāṉ.
He who irrigated the low ground takes bis share of the produce.
4579. paḷḷam uḷḷa iṭattilē taṇṇīr niṟkum.
Water will stand in a hollow.
4580. paḷḷik kaṇakkup puḷḷikku utavātu.
Accounts learnt at school will not be practically useful.
4581. paḷḷik kuppattukku ampaṭṭa vāttiyār.
A barber school master for a village inhabited by Pallis.
4582. paḷḷi pākkut tiṉṟāl pattu viralum cuṇṇāmpu.
If a Palli chewsbetel, his ten fingers will be smeared with chunam.
4583. paḷḷip piḷḷi eṉṟāl celvam kuṟiyumā?
If you admit that you are a school boy, will your wealth decrease?
4584. paḷḷikku vikkāmaṟ koḷḷikkuk kuṟittu vittār.
My father not only neglected to put me to school, but left me to bear
the expenses of the fire brand.
Under this proverb a general note on funeral rites may not be out of
place. The particulars given relate to Hindu families generally, not to
brahmans.
When a person dies, the grief of the females in the family bursts out
into loud lamentations. On its being made known by a conch blower that a
death has occurred, the neighboring females go to the house, add the
expression of their sympathy, and all unite in one general wail.
Frequently one of the company, the mother, or it may be a professional
person, breaks out into an ascription of prise regarding the
deceaseduttering a sort of elegy in measured cadence. In this case when
a climax is reached, all join in a chorus of grief, and some may smite
their breasts and tear their hir. In such elegies, which are often of a
touching kind, the personal beauty, the talents, the learning and the
prowess of the deceased are diveltion(?).
The body being taken out is bathed and dressed as in life. In the case
of females the gold and pearls and precious stones, if she possessed
them, will be put on. The sectarial marks will be added. The charpoy on
which the corpse bas been lid out is then carried into the house. Agin
the females seat themselves around it and pour forth a torrent of grief,
or at any rate make a loud noise. All kinsman whose business it is to
attend to the customary observance spreads a cloth on the charpoy to
hold rice, called,-rice for the mouth. The females of the family take a
little of the grin and put it on the corpse near the mouth. The same
ceremony is performedby the male members of the family at the place of
cremation. The family barber appropriates the remining rice. At the
burning ground the kinsman who conducts the ceremonies carries a pot of
water round the pyre and the conch-blower follows him and makes an
incision on the pot with his conch, when the barber puts four bits of
sandal or other wood into the hands of the kinsman and points out the
places in the pyre where they are to be inserted. The leaking pot is the
taken by the barber to be broken near the head of the corpse. The
kinsman hastens away that he may not hear the noise of the pot when
broken, nor see the burning pyre. Then the barber receive permission to
break the put and to ignite the pyre.
On the next or on an early day the male friends of the deceased assemble
at the place of cremation. The skull, the breast-bone and the bones of
the hands having been abstracted from the ashes deposited in a
convenient place covered with a plantin leaf, and the nearest of kin
smeared with oil, ghee and honey and covered in flowers, whilst a priest
recites appropriate incantations in the presence of the relatives. A pot
of milk havingbeen provided for this ceremony, therelatives take a sprig
of coronilla grandiflora and having dippedit, in the milky sprinkle the
relics, which are then putinto an unburnt earthen vessel, covered with
cloth to be cast into the sacred stream or bestowed on the ocean wave.
Of course these elaborate rites are dispensed withby thepoor.
I have seen Hindus, not by any means in abject circumstances convey a
corpse to the seaside, where a grave had been hastily dug and take off
the clothand ornaments, wrap the body in a common mud(?) and bury it
with the burial of a dog. In the year 1830 when travelling in Bengal I
saw, on the bank of the Damooda, a sorrowing brahmanical father with two
or three attendants dispose of a corpse with but little ceremony. The
father himself who was much affected ignited the pyre.
4585. paḷḷi oḷittirāṉ pārppāṉ kuḷittirāṉ.
A palli does not like to be secluded, nor does a brahman fast after
bathing.
4586. paṟakkiṟa paṟavikku etu tūram ?
What is distancetoabird on the wing?
4587. paṟakkum kākam irukkum kompu aṟiyātatu pōla.
As a crow on the wing knows not on what branch itwill alight.
4588. paṟakkum kuruvikku irukkum komputeriyātu, paratēcikkut taṅkum iṭam
teriyātu.
A bird on the wing knows not the branch on which it may alight,a
stranger knows not his halting place.
4589. paṟantu pōkiṟa ecciṟ kalli mēl kallit tūkki vittāṟpōla.
Like one placing a stone an a flying leaf that has been used for a
rice-plate.
4590.paṟappāṉ payir iḻantāṉ. aṟakkāñci peṇṭuiḻantāṉ.
A hasty man loses the produce of this field, and the jealous man his
wife.
4591. paṟitta kāṭṭukkup payam illi.
No further fear in a jungle, where one has been already robbed.
4592. paṟicci piḷḷiyip paḷḷikku vittālum pēccilē ayyē eṉṉumām.
Though a pariah child be sent to school, he will still call his father
ayye.
4593. paṟi taṭṭiṉāṟpōla.
Like striking grin to the level of the measure.
4594. paṟit teruvilē vilvam muḷittatupōla.
As a vilva tree sprang up in a pariah street.
4595. paṟippāṭṭukkum paṟip pēccukkum curippūviṟkum maṉam illi
A pariah's song, his dialect, and a suri flower, have no fragrance in
them.
4596. paṟiyaṉum pārppāṉum pōla.
Like a pariah and a brahman.
4597. paṟiyaṉ poṅkal iṭṭāl pakavāṉukku ēṟātō?
If a pariah boil rice as an offering to God, will it not be accepted?
4598. paṟiyi paḷḷikku vittālum tuṟip pēccup pōmā?
Though a pariah is schooled, will his vulgar brogue be altered?
4599. paṟivēli arivēli.
The work of a pariah is only half done.
4600. paṉaṅkāṭṭu nari calacalappukku añcumā?
Will the jackal of the palmyrah grove tremble at the rustling of leaves?
4601. paṉikkaṇ tiṟantāl maḻikkaṇ tiṟakkum.
If there be dew, there will be no rin.
4602. paṉi peytu kaṭal niṟiyumā ?
Will the sea be filled by the falling dew?
4603. paṉi peytu kuḷam nirampumā?
Will the falling dew fill the tank?
4604. paṉi peytāl maḻi illi, paḻam iruntāl pū illi.
When there is dew there is no rin, when fruit is ripe there is no
blossom.
4605. paṉip perukkilē kappal ōṭṭalāmā ?
Can you sil a ship in heavy dew?
4606. paṉiyi nampi ērppūṭṭiṉatu pōla.
Like beginning to plough depending on the dew.
4607. paṉi ēṟiyum pāḷi toṭātu iṟaṅkiṉāṟpōla.
Like descending from a palmyrah tree without touching the flower spathe.
4608. paṉi ēṟi viḻuntavaṉik kaṭā ēṟi mitittatu pōla.
Like a buffalo trampling on a person who has fallen from a palmyrah
tree.
4609. paṉi marattiṉ kīḻē pālik kuṭittālum kaḷ eṉṟu niṉippār.
Though what you drink in a palmyrah grove be milk, every one will
believe it to be toddy.
4610. paṉimarattiṟku niḻal illi paṟiyaṉukku muṟi illi.
A palmyrah tree casts no shade, a pariah has no rules.
4611. paṉi maram ēṟukiṟavaṉi etuvariyum tāṅkalām?
How far can you support a man who is climbing a palmyrah tree?
4612. paṉiyiṉ niḻalum niḻalō pakivar uṟavum uṟavō?
Is the shadow of a palmyrah tree a shade, is the acquaintance of an
enemy friendship?
4613. paṉiyil iruntu viḻuntavaṉip pāmpu kaṭittatu pōl.
Like a snake biting one who has fallen from a palmyrah tree.
4614. paṉi veṭṭiṉa iṭattilē kaḻuti vaṭṭam pōṭṭatu pōla.
Like an ass going round a place where a palmyrah tree had been felled.
4515. paṉṟikkuṭṭikku orucanti ētu?
Does a young pig observe fasts?
4516. paṉṟikkuṭṭi āṉi āmā?
Will the young pig become an elephant?
4617.paṉṟi paṭṭāl avaṉōṭē, kāṭṭāṉi paṭṭāl paṅku.
If a hog be shot he takes the whole, if an elephant be shot I shall have
a share.
4618. paṉṟi pala kuṭṭi, ciṅkam oru kuṭṭi.
The sow has many young ones at a time, the lioness only one.
4619. paṉṟi pala kuṭṭi pōṭṭu eṉṉa?
What, if a sow has a numerous litter?
4620. paṉṟiyōṭu kūṭiya kaṉṟum malam tiṉṉum.
A calf that goes with a pig will eat excrement.
4621. paṉṉakkāraṉ peṇṭil paṇiyak kiṭantu cettāḷām.
It is sid that the wife of a mat-maker died on the bare ground.
4622. paṉṉip paṉṉip paḻaṅkati paṭiyātē.
Do not tell old stories with affectation.
4623. pākaṟ koṭṭi putikkac curik koṭṭi muḷikkumā?
Willa bottle gourd spring from a pagal seed ?
4624. pācam aṟṟavaṉ paratēci.
He who has no ties is like a foreigner.
4625. pāṭakakkāli vāḻntāl pattu eṭṭuc caṉam piḻikkum.
If the foot-ringed lady prosper, eight or ten people may be supported
thereby.
4626. pāṭam ēṟiṉum ēṭu kiviṭēl.
Though you have learnt your lesson, do not throw away your book.
4627. pāṭip pāṭik kuttiṉālum pataril arici illi.
Though pounded with a song, chaff will not yield rice.
4628. pāṭu eṉṟāl pāṇaṉum pāṭāṉ.
When pressed to sing, even the professional singer refuses.
4629. pāṭu paṭāmaṟ pōṉāl palaṉ illāmaṟ pōkum.
Where there is no labour, there is no profit.
4630. pāṭum pulavar kiyiṟ paṭṭōli āṉēṉē.
I am become an ola in the band of a poet.
4631. pāṭi ēṟiṉum ōṭu kiviṭēl.
Though you may ascend the bier, forsake not your alms-dish.
4632. pāṇam toṭuttāṟ pōlap pēcukiṟāṉ.
He speaks as if arrows were darting forth.
4633. pātakar paḻakkam pāmpoṭu paḻakal pōla.
Friendship with the wicked, is like playing with snakes.
4634. pātic curikkāy kaṟikkum pātic curikkāy vitikkumā?
Can you take half a churikay for curry, and use the other half as seed?
4635. pāttiram aṟintu picci iṭu, kōttiram aṟintu peṇ koṭu.
Give alms to the worthy, and your dughter to one of a good family.
4636. pāppukku mūppu illi.
Brahmans have no chiefs.
4637. pāmaṇi āṟṟilē palli viḷakku, mulli āṟṟilē mukam kaḻuvu.
Begin to clean your teeth atPamani river, wash your face in Mulli
river.
4638. pāmpāṭṭi pāmpilē , kaḷḷaṉ kaḷavilē.
A snake charmer understands snakes, a thief understands thieving.
4639. pāmpilum pāmpukkuṭṭi viṣam atikam vīriyamum atikam.
A young snake is more poisonous and vigorous than an old one.
4640. pāmpiṉ kuṭṭi pāmpu ataṉ kuṭṭi naṭṭuvākkāli.
The young one of a snake is a snake, and its young one is a scorpion.
4641. pāmpiṉ vāyt tēripōla.
Like a toad in the jaws of a snake.
4642. pāmpukku mūppu illi.
Snakes have no chiefs.
4643. pāmpu kaṭikkat tēḷukkup pārkkiṟatō?
When bitten by a snake, will the incantations suited to scorpion bites
avil aught?
4644. pāmpukku irācā mūṅkil taṭi.
A bambu stick is king to a snake.
4645. pāmpukkuc catturu pañcamā?
Are the enemies of snakes few?
4646. pāmpukkut taliyik kāṭṭi mīṉukku vālik kāṭṭi.
Showing the head to snakes, and the til to fish.
Spoken of a two-faced person, in allusion to an eel that shows its
serpent-like head to snakes, and its fish-like til to fish.
4647. pāmpukkup pāl vārttu vaḷarttālum viṣattik koṭukkum.
Though you feed a snake with milk, it will yield poison.
4648. pāmpukkup pāl vārttatu pōla.
Like pouring out milk to a snake.
4649. pāmpum kīriyum pōla.
Lik a snake and a mungoose.
4650. pāmpum kīriyum pōlap pala kālam vāḻntēṉ.
I lived a long time with him or her as a snake with a mungoose.
4651. pāmpu taṉ paciyi niṉikkum tēri taṉ vitiyi niṉikkum.
The snake has regard to its hunger, the frog thinks of its fate.
4652. pāmpu pacikkil tēriyip piṭikkum. atupōla, ciṟiyōr ciṟiya kāriyaṅkaḷiyē
ceyvār.
When a snake is hungry it will catch a frog, so the vile perform only
mean acts.
4653. pāmpu tiṉkiṟa ūrilē pōṉāl naṭu muṟi namakku eṉṟu irukkavēṇṭum.
If we go to a country of snake eaters, we must be prepared for the
middle bit.
4654. pāmpu pakiyum tōl uṟavumā?
Is the snake hostile, and its skin friendly?
4655. pāmpukkup paki karuṭaṉ.
The hawk is inimical to the snake.
4656. pāmpum nōvāmaṟ pāmpu aṭitta kōlum nōvāmal irukkavēṇṭum.
So strike that neither the snake, nor the staff with which you strike,
shall be hurt or injured.
4657. pāmpu eṉṟāl paṭiyum naṭuṅkum.
Even an army will tremble at the word snake.
4658. pāmpoṭu paḻakēl.
Have nothing to do with snakes.
4659. pāykiṟa māṭṭukku muṉṉē vētam coṉṉāṟpōla.
Like reciting a portion of the vedas, to a cow about to gore you.
4660. pāymaram cērnta kākam pōl āṉēṉ.
I have become like a crow on the top of a mast.
4661. pāymaram illā marakkalam pōla.
Like a vessel without a mast.
4662. pāraputti uḷḷa paṟavi patarāl piṭikkappaṭṭatu.
A wise bird has been cught with chaff.
4663. pārāta uṭimi pāḻ.
Property not looked after perishes.
4664. pāṟikku ūṭāṭāp pāṟi pacumarattiṉ vērukku ūṭāṭi viṭum.
The rock that resists a crowbar, gives way to the roots of a tender
plant.
4665. pār āḷalāmeṉṟu pāl kuṭikkiṟāy.
Thou drinkest milk, hoping to govern the world.
4666. pārkkak koṭutta paṇattukku veḷḷikkiḻamiyā?
Is Friday a sufficient excuse for not returning the coin given you to
look at?
It is believed by many that, though it is fortunate to receive money on
Friday, it is unfortunate to pay it.
4667. pārkkiṟa kaṇṇukkuk kēṭkiṟa cevi pollātu.
A listening ear, is worse than a seeing eye.
4668. pārtta kaṇṇum pūttup pakalum iravu āyiṟṟu.
The eyes have failed by looking too long, and night has followed the
day.
4669. pārtta mukam ellām vēṟṟu mukam.
All the faces we see are diverse from one another.
4670. pārttāṟ pacuppōla, pāyntāl pulippōla.
In appearance like a cow, in action like a tiger.
4671. pārttirukkat tiṉṟu viḻittirukkak ki kaḻuvuvāṉ.
Gazed at-by starving beggars-he eats and washes his hands.
4672. pārttiruntavaḷ pacci kuttiṉāḷ, kēṭṭiruntavaḷ vaṟuttuk kuttiṉāḷ.
She who had seen the process pounded it undried, she who be heard of it
pounded roasted.
4673. pārppataṟku ariya parap piramam.
It is impossible to comprehend the supreme Brahma.
4874. pārppātti ammā māṭu vantatu.
Brahman matron, the cow has come.
4675. pārppātti uppukkaṇṭam pōkkaṭittatu pōla.
As a brahman woman lost her salted mutton.
4676. pārppār cēvakamum veḷḷik kutiric cēvakamum ākātu.
The service of a brahman, and the cate of a white horse are bad.
4677. pārppāṉukku vāypōkkātē āṇṭikku atutāṉum collātē.
Do not waste your breath on a brahman, nor converse with a mendicant.
4678. pārppāṉ kaṟuppum paṟiyaṉ civappum ākātu.
A black brahman, and a fair pariah are not trustworthy.
4679. pārppāṉ ēḻiyō pacu ēḻiyō?
Which is the more helpless, a brahman or a cow?
4680. pālarukkup palam aḻuki, maṭcattukkup palam utakam.
The strength of a child is crying, and that of fish is water.
4681. pālar moḻi kēḷātār kuḻal iṉitu yāḻ iṉitu eṉpār.
Those who have not heard the lisping of their own children say, that the
flute is sweet, the stringed instrument is sweet.
4682. pālāṉa neñcu ellām paki ākkiṉāṉ.
He has made enemies of all whose hearts are pure as milk.
4683. pālukkum kāval pūṉikkum tōḻaṉ.
He is the guard of the milk, and also the friend of the cat.
4684. pālukku vanta pūṉi mōrik kuṭikkumā ?
Will the cat that has come for new milk, drink buttermilk?
4685. pālukkuc cīṉi illi eṉṟōrkkum kūḻukkuk kaṟi illi eṉṟōrkkum vicāram
oṉṟē.
The care of those who want sugar with milk, and of those who want curry
for their rice, are the same.
4686. pālumām maruntumām.
It is of use both as milk and medicine.
4687. pālum patakku, mōrum patakkō ?
Is the rate of fresh milk that of buttermilk?
4688. pālum veḷḷi mōrum veḷḷi.
Milk is white and buttermilk is also white.
4689. pāli ūṭṭuvārkaḷ pākkiyatti ūṭṭuvārkaḷā?
They may feed him with milk; can they feed him with good fortune?
4690. pālikkuṭittavaṉukkup pāl ēppam varum, kaḷḷik kuṭittuvaṉukkuk kaḷ ēppam
varum.
He who has drank milk will belch milk, and he who has drank a toddy will
belch toddy.
4691. pālip pārkkiṟatā pāṉiyip pārkkiṟatā?
Do you examine the milk or the vessel contining it?
4692. pāloṭu kalanta nīrum pālākum.
Water mixed with milk looks like milk.
4693. pālōṭāyiṉum kālam aṟintu uṇ.
Though with milk, take your meals at the proper time.
4694. pāl āriyaṉukku, pacu rāmanātacuvāmikku.
Milk to an Aryan, and a cow for Ramanadaswami.
4695. pāl irukkiṟatu pākkiyam irukkiṟatu, pālilē pōṭṭu kuṭikkap pattup
parukkikku vaḻi illi.
There is milk and money, but no means for procuring ten grins of rice.
4696. pāl toṭṭup pāl kaṟakkavēṇṭum.
One must draw milk after moistening the fingers with milk.
4697. pāl nakkāta pūṉiyum paritāṉam vāṅkāta pirāmaṇaṉum uṇṭā?
Is there a cat that will not lap milk, or a brahman that will refuse
a bribe?
4698. pāl pacuvik kaṉṟilē teriyum, pākkiyavāṉ piḷḷiyi mukattilē teriyum.
The milch cow is known by its calf, and the child of the wealthy by its
face.
4699. pāl vārttu muḻukuvāṉ.
He will bathe after applying milk to the head.
Intimating that a person inimical has been got rid of.
4700. pāvaṭṭam pūppōla narittum putti illi.
Though as grey as a pavattam flower, he has no sense.
4701. pāvattiṟku iṭam koṭātavaṉ pāvattic ceyaṅkoḷvāṉ.
He who does not give place to sin, will conquer it.
4702. pāvam ceyyātiru, maṉamē!
O my heart, do not sin !
4703. pāvalar arumi nāvalar aṟivār.
The ability of a poet is best known to the learned.
4704. pāviyār pōṉa iṭam ellām paḷḷamum tiṭṭiyum.
Whithersoever the simple go, they meet with ups and downs.
4705. pāviyip piṭittup pāmpu āṭṭukiṟatu.
Seizing a simpleton and making him dance like a snake.
4706. pāvil irukkiṟatu pārttum irukkiṟatu.
That which is described in poetry is also seen in nature.
4707. pāḻāyp pōkiṟatu pacuviṉ vāyilē.
That which is spoiled is put in the mouth of a cow.
4708. pāḻ ūrukku nari rājā.
The jackal is king in a deserted village.
4709. pāṉiyil uṇṭāṉāl akappiyil varum.
If in the pot, it will come into the spoon.
4710. pāṉiyilē patakku nel iruntāl mūliyilē mukkuṟuḷi teyvam kūttāṭum.
If there be a pathaku of rice in the pot, three kurunies of gods will
dance in the corner.
4711. piciṉi taṉṉi vacaṉippatu vīṇ.
It is useless to extol a miser.
4712. piccaṉ vāḻit tōṭṭattilē pukuntatu pōla.
As a madman entered the plantin grove.
4713. piccikkāraṉukkup payappaṭṭu aṭuppu mūṭṭāmal viṭukiṟatā?
Through fear of beggars do you refrin from lighting your fire?
4714. piccikkāraṉi aṭittāṉām cōḷiyip pōṭṭu uṭittāṉām.
It is sid that he beat the mendicant and broke his alms-dish.
4715. piccikkāraṉ cōṟṟilē caṉīccuraṉ pukuntatu pōla.
As Saturn entered into the rice of the mendicant.
4716. piccic cōṟṟilum ecciṟ cōṟā?
What! scrupulous about leavings in rice got by begging?
4717. piccikkāraṉ mēlē piramāstiram toṭukkiṟatā?
Is an enchanted arrow discharged at a mendicant?
4718. piccikku vantavaṉ peṇṇukku māppiḷḷiyā?
Do you propose giving your dughter in marriage to one who came to ask
alms?
4719. piccikku mūttatu kaccavaṭam.
Merchandize is a little better than begging.
4720. piccic cōṟṟilum kuḻinta cōṟā?
Is rice offered in charity refused becuse it is overboiled?
4721. piccic cōṟṟiṟkup pañcam uṇṭā ?
Is rice given in charity ever scarce?
4722. picci pōṭṭatu pōtum nāyik kaṭṭu.
The alms given are sufficient; tie up the dog.
4723. picci pukiṉum kaṟki naṉṟē.
Learning is good even when one is reduced to begging.
4724. picci iṭṭuk keṭṭavaṉum uṇṭā ?
Has any one been ruined by giving alms ?
4725. picci iṭṭuk keṭṭavaṉum illi, piḷḷi peṟṟuk keṭṭavaṉum illi. None is
ruined by giving alms; none is ruined becuse he has a family.
4726. picci eṭuttum catturuviṉ kuṭi keṭu.
Though you have to beg, destroy the family of your enemy.
4727. piṭāram periteṉṟu puṟṟilē ki vikkalāmā?
Relying on the efficacy of the prescribed remedy, will you put your hand
in a snake hole?
4728. piṭāraṉukku añcip pāmpu elikku uṟavu āccutām.
It is sid that a snake afrid of the charmer, sought the friendship of
a rat.
4729. piṭāriyārē kaṭā vantatu.
O, demon, the bull has come.
4730. piṭāri varam koṭuttālum ōccaṉ varam koṭukkiṟatu aritu.
Though a pidari may grant a favour, it will be difficult to obtin it
through the priest.
4731. piṭi aḻaki pukuntāl peṇ aḻaki āvāḷ.
If a woman ornamented with jewels enter, she will be regarded a a
beautiful woman.
4732. piṭikkuppiṭi namacivāyamā.
Are incantations to be used agin and agin.
4733. piṭitta kompum viṭṭēṉ mititta kompum viṭṭēṉ.
I have left the branch I had seized, and also the one on which I was
standing.
4734. piṭitta kompum oṭintu mititta kompum muṟintāṟpōl āṉēṉ.
I have become as helpless as if the branch I seized, and the one I stood
upon, both broke at the same time.
4735. piṭittāṟ kaṟṟi, viṭṭāṟ kūḷam.
If tied, a bundle; if loose, bits of straw.
4736. pikṣāpatiyō lakṣāpatiyō?
Is he the prince of beggars, or the first as possessing lace?
4737. piṇatti mūṭi maṇattic cey.
Bury the corpse, and then celebrate the marriage.
4738. piṇam pōkiṟa iṭattē tukkamum pōkiṟatu.
Sorrow goes away to the place whither the corpse has gone.
4739. piṇippaṭṭuk koḷḷātē perumpavatti uttarippāy.
Do not stand security, it will lead to endless evils.
4740. piṇṭattukkuk kiṭiyātatu teṇṭattukku akappaṭum.
That which cannot be obtined for sustenance, will be found to pay a
fine.
4741. pittam pattu vitam.
Madness is of ten kinds i. e., many kinds.
4742. pittaḷi nāṟṟam aṟiyātu.
Brass is innocent of its own odour.
4743. pittarkkuc cila puttikaḷ coṉṉāl pēccik kēṭpārā?
If advice be given to fools will they listen to it?
4744. pittaṉukkut taṉ kuṇam nūliṉum cevvi.
The madman thinks his own character strighter than a line.
4745. piramacāri ōṭam kaviḻttatu pōla.
As the brahmachari upset the boat.
4746. piramā niṉittāl āyucukkuk kuṟiyā?
If Brahma wills it, is there any chance of your life being short ?
4747. piḻikkappōṉa iṭattilē piḻimōcam vantatupōla.
As a grave occurrence befell one in the place to which he had gone for a
livelihood.
4748. piḷḷikkāraṉ piḷḷikku aḻukiṟāṉ paṇi ceyvōṉ kācukku aḻukiṟāṉ.
The parent weeps on account of his child, the servant weeps for his
hire.
4749. piḷḷikku viḷiyāṭṭuc cuṇṭelikkup pirāṇa caṅkaṭam.
That which is sport to the child, is death to the mouse.
4750. piḷḷi patiṉāṟu peṟuvāḷeṉṟu eḻuti iruntālum, puruṣaṉ illāmal eppaṭip
peṟuvāḷ?
Though it were written in the horoscope that she would have sixteen
children, how could that be without a husband?
4751. piḷḷi peṟṟavaṉukkum māṭu paṭittavaṉukkum veṭkam illi .
A parent and a cowherd know no shame.
4752.piḷḷi peṟṟavaḷip pārttup perumūccu viṭṭu āvatueṉṉa ?
What if you look at her who has borne a child, and sigh?
4753. piḷḷi arumi malaṭi aṟivāḷā?
Does a barren woman understand the endearments of a child?
4754. piḷḷiyārukkup peṇ koḷvatu pōla.
Like obtining a wife for Ganesha.
4755. piḷḷiyārip piṭitta caṉi aracamarattiyum piṭittatupōla.
As Saturn who had seized Ganesha seized also the tree-ficus Indica.
4756. piḷḷiyār kōvilil tiruṭaṉ iruppāṉ, coṉṉālum kōḷ.
The thief has taken refuge in the temple of Pilliyar, it would how-ever
be a slander to mention it.
4757. piḷḷiyār piṭikkak kuraṅkāy muṭintatu.
The attempt to form an image of Ganesha, ended in the formation of a
monkey.
4758.piḷḷi illā vīṭṭil kiḻavaṉ tuḷḷi viḷiyāṭukiṟāṉām.
It is sid that in the house in which there are no children, an old man
plays likea child.
4759. piḷḷiyum puḻukkiyum cari, piḷḷikkum puḻukkikkum iṭam koṭātē.
A child and a slave are alike, be not indulgent to a child or a slave.
4760. piḷḷiyum kiḷḷit toṭṭilum āṭṭukiṟatā?
Do you rock the cradle, while pinching the child?
4761. piḷḷiyic cākkiṭṭup pūtam viḻuṅkukiṟatu.
Pretending to feed the child, the goblin swallows it.
4762. piḷḷi varattiṟkup pōy puruṣaṉip paṟikoṭuttatu pōla.
Like losing her husband, when she went to ask the gift of offspring.
4763. piḷḷi varuttam peṟṟavaḷukkut teriyum maṟṟavaḷukkut teriyumā?
A mother knows the pin of travil, is it known to others?
4764. piḷḷi vīṭṭukkārar cammatittāl pāti vivākam muṭintatu pōla.
If the family of the bridegroom consent, half the ceremony of marriage
is over.
4765. piṟakkiṟapoḻutē muṭam āṉāl pēykku iṭṭup paṭittāl tīrumā?
If a person be a cripple from his birth, can he be cured by offerings
made to demons?
4766. piṟanta piḷḷi piṭi cōṟṟiṟku aḻukiṟatu, piṟakkappōkiṟa piḷḷikkut taṇṭi
cataṅki tēṭukiṟārkaḷām.
It is sid that they are making silver bells for the child about to be
born, while the child on the lap is crying for a handful of rice.
4767. piṟantavaṉ iṟappatē nicam.
It is certin that he who is born will die.
4768. piṟantaṉa iṟakkum tōṉṟiṉa maṟiyum.
Those who are born will die, what is visible will vanish.
4769. piṟanta aṉṟē iṟakkavēṇṭum.
The day of birth leads to death.
4770. piṟanta ūrukkuc cēli vēṇṭām, peṇṭu irunta ūrukkut tāli vēṇṭām.
A sumptuous cloth is not required in one's native village, nor a tali
where one is known as a wife.
4771. piṟar poruḷi iccippāṉ taṉ poruḷi iḻappāṉ.
He who covets the property of others, will lose his own.
4772. piṟarmaṉit turumpu koḷḷāṉ pirāmaṇaṉ taṇṭu koṇṭāṉ.
He who would not carry off a rush belonging to another's roof, robbed a
brahman his master of his staff.
4773. piṟavic ceviṭaṉukkup pēcat tiṟamuṇṭā ?
Are there any clever of speech who were born deaf?
4774. piṟavik kuruṭaṉukkuk kaṇ kiṭittatu pōla.
As one born blind received sight.
4775. piṟaṉ maṉi pukāmi aṟam eṉat takum.
Not going to another man's house, deserves to be called virtue.
4776. piṉṉālē varum palākkāyiṉum muṉṉālē varum kaḷākkāy nalam.
A kalakkay is better in hand, than a jack fruit in prospect.
4777. piṉṉi eṉpatum pēcātiruppatum, illi eṉpataṟku aṭiyāḷam.
By and by, and silence, are tantamount to a refusal.
4778. pīram pēṇi pāram tāṅku.
Preserve your strength and bear the burden.
4779. pīṟiṉa puṭivi perunāḷ irātu.
A ragged cloth will not wear long.
4780. pīṟṟaṟ paṭṭikku aṟutaṟkoṭi.
A broken cord, and a ragged basket (well-bucket.)
4781. pukaḻcciyāṉukku īntatu pūtakkaṇṇāṭi.
That which was given to the prise-worthy, is a microscope.
4782. pukaḻntārip pōṟṟi vāḻ.
Defend those who acknowledge your merit.
4783. pukikkiṉum kārakil pollāṅku eḻātu.
Though eaglewood produces smoke, it will do no harm.
4784. puṅkappukaḻē taṅkattikaḻē.
The contempt of riches is the highest prise.
4785. puṇṇiyattukkuk kiṇaṟu veṭṭap pūtam puṟappaṭṭatu pōla.
As a demon came out of a well that had been dug as an act of religious
merit.
4786. puṇṇiyattukku uḻuta māṭṭip pallip piṭittup pārttatupōla.
Like looking at the teeth of a bullock that is ploughing for nothing.
4787. puṇṇiyam pārkkap pōyp pāvam piṉṉē vantatām.
It is sid that evil followed the man who attempted a kind act.
4788. puṇṇiyam illāta vaḻikāṭṭi vīṇ.
A leader not virtuous is worthless.
4789. puṇṇilē kōl iṭṭatu pōla.
Like thrusting a stick into a wound.
4790. puṇṇilē puḷi paṭṭatupōlē.
As if a sore had become acidified.
4791. puṇṇukkō maruntukkō vīcam?
Whether calls for immediate action, the sore or its remedy?
4792. putiya kāriyaṅkaḷiṟ putiya yōcaṉi vēṇṭum.
New things require fresh consideration.
4793. putiya vaṇṇāṉum paḻiya ampaṭṭaṉum tēṭa vēṇṭum.
Employ a new washerman, but an old barber.
4794. putiya vaṇṇāṉ pontukaṭṭi veḷuppāṉ.
A new washerman will wash with great care.
4795. putiyāri nampip paḻiyārik kiviṭalāmā?
Is it right to forsake old friends in reliance un pew ones?
4796. putuk kuṭattilē vārtta taṇṇīr.
Water poured into a new pot.
4797. putup pāṉikku ī cērātu.
Flies do not swarm on anewpot.
4798. putup peṇṇē putup peṇṇē neruppu eṭuttuvā, uṉakkup piṉṉālē irukkiṟatu
ceruppu aṭi.
O bride, O bride bring some fire, what awaits you is slippering.
4799.putumiyāṉa kāriyam tāṉ intak kaliyukattil.
Certinlyit is a strange occurrence in kaliyuga.
4800. putumikku vaṇṇāṉ paṟi taṭṭi veḷuttāṉ.
The washerman inugurated his washing by beat of tomtom.
4801. putiyal eṭuttavaṉippōlē.
Like one that has discovered buried treasure.
4802.putti keṭṭa rājāvukku mati keṭṭa mantiri.
A foolish king and an ignorant minister.
4803. putti aṟṟōrum tūṅkiṉar, putti uṟṟōrum tūṅkiṉar.
Both the foolish and the wise slept.
4804. puttimāṉ palavāṉ.
An intelligent man is strong.
4805. putti muṟṟiṉavarkkuc cittiyātatu oṉṟum illi.
There is nothing in which the mature in judgment fail.
4806. putti uṟap pukaḻ.
As soundness of judgment increases, prise will follow.
4807. putti aṟṟavarkaḷ pattiyāyc ceyvatum viparītamām.
Even that which foolsperform earnestly is wrong.
4808. putti īṉarkaḷ kuḷikkap pōyc cēṟṟip pūcikkoḷvārkaḷ.
When the foolish go to wash, they will dub themselves with mud.
4809. puraṭṭip puraṭṭip utikkiṟapōtum, mīciyil maṇ paṭavilli eṉkiṟāṉ.
Even when kicked about and rolling in the dust, he affirms that his
mustache did not touch the earth.
4810. puruṣaṉ aṭikkak koḻuntaṉ kōpittatu pōla.
Like the younger brother of the husband, rebuking him for beating his
wife.
4811. puruṣaṉukku ēṟṟa mārāppu.
The cloth covering the breast suited to the rank of her husband.
4812. puruvattiṟ paṭṭāl karikkumō kaṇṇil paṭṭāl karikkumō ?
When does acid cuse smarting, when it is applied to the eye-brow or the
eyes?
4813. puli aṭikku muṉṉē kili aṭikkum.
Struck with fear before struck by the tiger.
4814. pulikkup piṟantu nakam illāmaṟ pōkumā?
Being born a tiger, will it be without claws?
4815. pulikkut taṉ kāṭu eṉṉa vēṟṟukkāṭu eṉṉa?
What matters it to the tiger whither he is in his native jungle or
another?
4816. pulikkup piṟantu pūṉiyāyp pōkumā?
Being born a tiger, will it become a cat ?
4817. pulik kūṭṭattil māṉ akappaṭṭatu pōla.
As the deer was cught among tigers.
4818. puvi cevi tirukiya mata kaḷiṟu.
An elephant in rut whose ears were wrung offbya tiger.
4819. puli nakam paṭāviṭṭālum, ataṉ mīci kuttiṉālum viṣam,
Though one may escape the claws of the tiger, even the pricking of its
whiskers will prove malignant.
4820. puli pacittāl pullut tiṉṉumā?
When the tiger is hungry, will he eat grass?
4821. puli patuṅkuvatu pāyccalukku iṭam.
The crouching of the tiger is the prelude to a spring.
4822. pulip pāl kuṭittavaṉ pōl irukkiṟāṉ.
He is like one fed on tiger's milk.
4823. puliyip pārttu nari cūṭu iṭṭukkoṇṭatupōla.
As the jackal branded itself in imitation of the stripes of a tiger.
4824. puliyaṉukku vākkuc cuttiyum āṇiyum illi.
The base do not talk decently, nor do they heed oaths.
4825. puliyum koliyum kaḷavum tavir.
Avoid lies, murder and theft.
4826. pul uḷḷa iṭattil māṭṭi āṭṭi mēya oṭṭātu.
It will not allow either sheep or cows to graze where there is grass.
4827. pullum pūmiyum uḷḷamaṭṭum.
As long as vegetation and the earth exist.
4828. pullum pūmiyum kallum kāvēriyum uḷḷa maṭṭum.
As long as vegetation, earth, rocks and the Cauvery exist.
4829. pullu viṟkiṟa kaṭiyilē pū viṟkiṟatu.
Selling flowers in a grass market.
4830. pullit tiṉṉum māṭupōlap puliyit tiṉṉum cennāy utavumā?
Will the red dog that eats tiger’s flesh prove as useful as the cow that
eats grass ?
4831. puḻukki kalam kaḻuvit tiṉṉātu.
The low-born doos not eat from a washed plate.
4832. puḻukki oḻukkam aṟiyumā piṇṇākkuc caṭṭi patam aṟiyumā?
Does the low-born appreciate good manners, does the oil cake pan know
when the cake is baked?
4833. puḻukki oḻukkam aṟiyātu pittaḷi nāṟṟam aṟiyātu.
Menials are ignorant of manners, brass is unconscious of its ill odour.
4834. puḻukkikkup putti piṭariyilē.
The sensibility of menials is in the neck.
4835. puḻuṅkip puḻuṅkip mā iṭittālum puḻukkiccikku oru koḻukkaṭṭi.
Though the slave girl who pounds rice laboursso as to perspire, her
share is only a cake.
4836. puḻutta carakkukkuk koḻutta paṇam.
Fat money for rotten articles.
4837. puḷiyamarattil ēṟiṉavaṉ pal kūciṉāl iṟaṅkuvāṉ.
He who has climbed a tamarind tree will come down when his tooth are set
on edge.
4838. puḷiyam paḻattukkup puḷippup pukutaviṭa varukiṟāyō?
Do you come to infuse acidity into the tamarind fruit?
4839. puḷukiṉālum poruntap puḷukavēṇṭum.
Even when boasting do it so as to secure belief.
4840. puḷḷik kaṇakkaṉ paḷḷikku āvāṉā?
Is a practical arithmetician fit to teach the theory of arithmetic ?
4841. puṟakkaṭi maruntu kavikku utavātu.
The medicinal plant in the backyard will not avil in an emergency.
4842. puṟakkuṭattut taṇṇīrpōla.
Like water on the outside of a pot.
4843. puṟṟilē ānti viḻippatu pōla.
Blinking like an owl on an ant-hill.
4844. puṟṟilē īcal puṟappaṭṭatu pōla.
Coming forth like a swarm of winged white ants from an ant-hill.
4845. puṟṟilē kiṭanta puṭiyaṉ eḻuppiṉatupōla.
Rushing as a beaver snake from its hole.
4846. puṟṟilē īcal puṟappaṭṭālum, maṇṇilē kaṟiyāṉ kūṭiṉālum maḻi varavē varum.
When winged white ants issue out of a hole, and white ants swarm it will
certinly rin.
4847. pū aracu irukkap poṉṉukku aḻuvāṉēṉ?
Why weep for gold while you have the tulip tree?
4848. pūcaṇikkāy attaṉi muttuk kātil ēṟṟukiṟatā mūkkil ēṟṟukiṟatā?
If a pearl be as large as a pumpkin, where is it to be worn, in the ear
or in the nose?
4849. pūcaṇikkāy eṭuttavaṉit tōḷilē kāṇalām.
If one has taken a pumpkin, its mark may be seen on his shoulder.
4850. pūcaṇikkāy aḻukiṉatupōla.
Like a decayed pumpkin.
4851. pūcap pūcap poṉ niṟam.
The more you gild it, the more like gold will it appear.
4852. pūcāri pū muṭikkap pōṉāṉām, pū ālaṅkāṭu palāk kāṭāyp pōccutām.
The priest began to wear flowers, and the flower garden became a grove
of jack trees.
4853. pūci mukattilē karaṭiyi viṭṭu ōṭṭiṉatu pōla.
Like driving a bear among those engaged in worship.
4854. pūcci marikkiṟatilli, puḻuvum cākiṟatilli.
Insects do not perish, nor do worms die.
4855. pūṭṭip pucikkāmaṟ putippār īyippōl īṭṭi iḻappār.
Those who lock up their treasure and refuse to enjoy it will be deprived
of it, as bees are deprived of their honey.
4856. pūṭṭum tiṟappum pōla.
As a lock and its key.
4857. pūtalam yāvum pōṟṟu muccuṭar.
The three lights which the whole world extol.
4858. pūttāṉamāṉa piḷḷi āttāḷit tāli kaṭṭiṟṟām.
It is sid that an indulged child tied a tali on his mother.
4859. pūttuc coriyap poṟuppārkaḷ pūṭṭik kaṭṭak kalaṅkuvārkaḷ.
They can endure seeing their tree shedding its blossoms, but will be
disquieted at seeing others string and wear there.
4860. pūmi atira naṭavāta puṇṇiyavāṉ.
A virtuous person under whose footsteps the earth trembles not.
4861. pūmi tirutti uṇ.
Till the soil, and enjoy its produce.
4862. pūrāṭakkāraṉōṭu pōrāṭa muṭiyātu.
One cannot strive with one born under the star puradam.
4863. pūriyōrkku illic cīriya oḻukkam.
The base are void of good manners.
4864. pūrvōttaram mēru kāttiram pōl irukkiṟatu.
His pedigree is weighty as mount Meru.
4865. pūlōkamutaliyār paṭṭam, pukuntu pārttāl poṭṭal.
His title is lord of the world, but when examined he is found empty.
4866. pū viṟkum kaṭiyil pul viṟpatupōla.
A grass is sold in a flower market.
4867. pū viṟṟa kaṭiyil pul viṟkalāmā?
May grass be sold in the flower market?
4868. pū viṟṟa kācu maṇakkumā pulāl viṟṟa kācu nāṟumā?
Is the money obtined from the sale of flowers fragrant, does that
obtined from the sale of flesh stink?
4869. pū viṟṟavaṉip poṉ viṟkappaṇṇuvēṉ.
I will enable him who sold flowers to sell gold.
4870. pūvuṭaṉ kūṭi nārum maṇam peṟṟatu pōla.
As a fibre used for stringing flowers partook of their fragrance.
4871. pūvuḷ maṅkiyām poṟkoṭiyām pōṉa iṭam ellām ceruppaṭiyām.
It is sid that she is Lackshmi residing in flowers, and she is a golden
creeper, yet whithersoever she goes she is slippered.
4872. pūṉi uḷḷa iṭattilē eli pēraṉ pētti eṭukkiṟatu.
The rat lives to see its grand-sons and grand-dughters in a place where
there is a cat.
4873. pūṉi kaṭṭum toḻuvattil āṉi kaṭṭalāmā?
Can you tether an elephant in a place suited for tying up a cat?
4874. pūṉikku illi tāṉamum tavamum.
Alms-giving andpenance are not prescribed to cats.
4875. pūṉikkuk koṇṭāṭṭam elikkut tiṇṭāṭṭam.
Amusement to the cat, and agony to the rat.
4876. pūṉikkuc cimāḷam vantāl pīṟṟaṟpāyil puraḷumām.
It is sid that if a cat be merry, she will roll about on an old mat.
4877. pūṉi pāl kuṭikkiṟatu pōla.
As a cat drinks milk.
4878. pūṉi pōl aṭaṅkiṉāṉ pulipōl pāyntāṉ.
He was quiet as a cat, and sprang like a tiger.
4879. pūṉiyik kaṇṭa kiḷipōlap pulampi aḻukiṟāṉ.
He cries as a parrot encountered by a cat.
4880. pūṉiyittāṉ vīṭṭup puliyeṉṟum, eli aracaṉeṉṟum
colvārkaḷ.
A cat is called a domestic tiger, and the king of rats.
4881. peṭṭiyiṟ pāmpupōl aṭaṅkiṉāṉ.
He was as quiet as a snake in a box.
4882. peṭṭi pīṟṟal vāyk kaṭṭut tiṟam.
The basket is torn, but the rim is strong.
4883. peṭṭik kōḻi kūviyō viṭikiṟatu?
Does the day break at the crowing of a hen?
4884. peṭṭik kōḻi taṭṭik kūvumā?
Can a hen flap her wings and crow like a cock?
4885. peṇ eṉṟāl pēyum iraṅkum.
Even a demon will pity a woman.
4886. peṇṇāci oru pakkam maṇṇāci oru pakkam.
Love of women on the one hand, and love of property on the other.
4887. peṇcāti illātavaṉ pēyik kaṭṭit taḻuviṉatupōla,
As the man who had no wife embraced a demoness.
4888. peṇcātiyit tāy vīṭṭil viṭṭavaṉukku oru coṭṭu.
A cuff for the man who left his wife at her mother's.
4889. peṇcāti kāṟkaṭṭu, piḷḷi vāykkaṭṭu.
Fettered with a wife, and muzzled with a child.
4890. peṇṭāṭṭi koṇṭatum tiṇṭāṭṭam paṭṭatum pōtum.
Enough of taking a wife and suffering the consequences.
4891. peṇṭirkku aḻaku pōcātiruttal.
Not to remonstrate with her husband is an ornament in a woman.
4892. peṇṭukaḷ cūttukkup purimaṇi.
A straw twist for women to squat on.
4893. peṇṭukaḷukkup peṟṟār iṭattilum piḷḷikaḷ iṭattilum mūppu illi.
Women have no influence over parents and children.
4894. peṇṭukaḷ cōṟṟukkut taṇṭam illi.
Women are not in danger of forfeiting their rice.
4895. peṇṇarampik kūttukkup pōyp pēyk kūttu āccutē.
The going to a dance of celestials ended as a dance of devils.
4896. peṇṇiṉ kuṇam aṟivēṉ campanti vāy aṟivēṉ.
I know the character of the bride, and the boisterousness of her
mother-in-law.
4897. peṇṇukkup poṉ iṭṭuppār , cuvarukku maṇ iṭṭuk
pār.
Look at a woman after adorning her with jewels, and at a wall when you
have plastered it with mud.
4898. peṇṇukkum poṉṉukkum tōṟpu uṇṭā?
Is any one ever tired of women and wealth?
4899. peṇṇukkup pōyp poṉṉukkup piṉvāṅkalāmā?
Having gone to take a wife, can you turn back becuse marriage is
expensive?
4900. peṇṇukku māmiyārum, piḷḷikku vāttiyārum.
For a wife, a mother-in-law, for a boy, a tutor.
4901. peṇṇik koṭuttavaḷō kaṇṇik koṭuttavaḷō?
Did she give one a wife, or did she give one her eye ?
4902. peṇṇip piḻi poṟukkap peṟṟa tāy vēṇṭāmō?
Are you content to lose your mother in order to pardon your wife?
4903. peṇṇik koṇṭu piyaṉ pēyāṉāṉ, piḷḷi peṟṟuc ciṟukki nāyāṉāḷ.
Having married a wife the boy has become a fool, having given birth to a
child the damsel has become mean in appearance.
4904. peṇṇi vēṇṭum eṉṟāl iḷiyaṟ kaṇṇi nakku.
If you want the woman, lick her bleared eyes.
4905. peṇ putti kēṭkiṟavaṉ pēy.
He who listens to the advice of women is a fool.
4906. peṇ putti piṉ putti.
The thoughts of women are after-thoughts.
4907. peṇ vaḷartti pīrkkaṅkoṭi.
The growing of women is that of a gourd creeper.
4908. periyārit tuṇikkoḷ.
Secure the good will of the great.
4909. periyōr ellām periyārum allar.
All who are high in stature are not great.
4910. periyōr muṉ tāḻntu pēcil, nāṇalippōl nimirntu koḷvārkaḷ.
When speaking submissively to the great, they preserve an erect posture
like a reed.
4911. periyōr muṉ etirttuppēcil, veḷḷattiṟku muṉ maraṅkaḷippōl vīḻvārkaḷ.
If they should contradict the great, they will fall like trees before a
flood.
4912. peruṅ kayiṟu muṭi aḻuntātu.
A tight knot cannot be formed in a thick rope.
4913. peruṅkāyam irunta pāṇṭam.
An earthen pot in which assafoetida was kept.
4914. peruṅ kāṟṟil pīḷippañcu paṟakkiṟatupōla.
Flying like cotton before a gale of wind.
4915. peruṅ kulattil piṟantālum putti aṟṟavaṉ karumpup pūp pōla.
Though born in a high family, a fool is like a sugar-cane flower.
4916. peruṅ koṭi piccikkārarukkut tuṇivu.
Beggars presume on large gifts.
4917. peru neruppukku īram uṇṭā ?
Will moisture affect a great fire?
4918. peru marattic cuṟṟiya vaḷḷikkoṭipōla.
Like a creeping plant-Dioscorea sativa-round a large tree.
4919. peru maḻi viḻuntāṟ kuḷirātu.
No feeling of cold in a heavy fall of rin.
4920. perumāḷ irukkiṟa variyil tirunāḷ uṇṭu.
As long as Perumal exists, holy days will be observed.
4921. perumāḷ eṉkiṟa peyari māṟṟap periya perumāḷ āccutu.
The name Perumal being changed has become great Perumal.
4922. perumāṉic cērntōrkkup piṟappu illi, picciccōṟṟiṟku eccil illi.
Those who have attined union with God are not subject to future births,
rice given in alms is not refused becuse it is refuse.
4923. perumāṉ niṉittāl vāḻvu kuṟivā, piramā niṉittāl āyucu kuṟivā?
If God is pleased, will there be any lack of prosperity, if Brahma
favour, will one's life be short?
4924. perumitāṉ arumiyi kulikkum.
Pride will diminish one's worth.
4925. perumiyāṉa tarittiraṉ vīṇ.
Pride in a poor man is vin.
4926. perumi oru muṟam puṭittu eṭuttāl oṉṟum illi.
When a sieve, full of pride, is sifted, nothing remins.
4927. perumiyum ciṟumiyum vāyāl varum.
The great and the little come from the mouth.
4928. perumikku āṭṭi aṭittup piḷḷi kiyil kātic cuṭṭuk koṭuttāṉ.
He killed a sheep to show his greatness, and gave the ear to his child
after roasting it.
4929. peru rūpatti uṭiyavarum pirayōcaṉamāy irukkamāṭṭār. atupōla, paṉi viti
peritāy iruntum niḻal koṭukkamāṭṭātu.
The great are not always helpful, the lofty palmyrah casts no shade.
4930. peru vayiṟu koṇṭatu aṟiyāmal cīmantattiṟku nāḷ iṭṭukkoṇṭāṉ.
Not knowing that his wife is affected with dropsy, he has fixed upon a
day for the performance of her simantham ceremony.
A ceremony relating to the first pregnancy, including bathing, the
parting of the hir in the middle of the forehead, putting jewels, &c.,
&c
4931. peṟṟa tāy iṭattilō kaṟṟa vitti kāṭṭukiṟāy?
Do you practise your arts on your mother ?
4932. peṟṟa tāy cettāl peṟṟa appaṉ ciṟṟappaṉ.
If a mother dies, the father becomes uncle.
4933. peṟṟa tāy pacittirukkap pirāmaṇa pōjaṉam ceytatu pōla.
Like feeding brahmans when one's mother is starving.
4934. peṟṟatu ellām piḷḷiyā iṭṭatu ellām payirā?
Are all that are brought forth children, is all that is sown avilable
for use?
4935. peṟṟatu ellām piḷḷiyō vaṉintatu ellām kucakkalamō ?
Are all that are brought forth children, is all earthen ware prefix that
is fashioned by a potter?
4936. peṟṟa maṉam pittu, piḷḷi maṉam kallu.
The mother's heart is soft, that of her child is as a stone.
4937. peṟṟōrkku illic cuṟṟamum ciṉamum.
The wise are not moved by relationship or anger.
4938. pēcap pēca entap pāṣiyum varum.
Any language may be learnt by continual speaking.
4939. pēcātiruntāl piḻi oṉṟum illi.
No fault arises if nothing is spoken.
4940. pēcil apalam pēcākkāl ūmi.
When he speaks it is to no purpose, when he does not speak he is
accounted dumb.
4941. pēccuk kaṟṟa nāy vēṭṭikku ākātu.
A noisy dog is not fit for hunting.
4942. pēccukku irāvaṇaṉ piṉpu kumpakarṇaṉ.
In speech he is Ravana, but he turns out to be Kumbhakarnaan.
4943. pēccukkup pēccuc ciṅkāramā?
Is contradiction becoming?
4914. pēcci viṟṟuk kāyccik kuṭikkiṟāṉ.
He sells his words, and cooks and drinks.
4945. pēṭi kiyil irunta āyutam pōla.
Like a weapon in the hands of a hermaphrodite.
4946.pēṭi kiyil vāḷpōlē.
Like a sword in the hands of a hermaphrodite.
4947. pētam aṟṟavar nītam uṟṟavar.
He who is impartial is just.
4948. pētikaḷ veḷḷattilē niṉṟum tākattiṟkut taṇṇīrukku alivārkaḷ.
Fools in the midst of a flood will wander about for water to drink.
4949. pētimi eṉpatu mātarkku aṇikalam.
Simplicity is the ornament of a woman.
4950. pēyāṉālum tāy, nīrāṉālum mōr.
Though like a demon she is a mother, though mere slop, it is
butter-milk.
4951. pēyum cilatu ñāyam pakarum.
Even a demon will have some reason to assign.
4952. pēy āṭiya kampam pōla .
Like a pole on which a demon dances.
4953. pēy koṇṭālum peṇ koḷḷal ākātu.
One may take a demon, but not take a wife.
4954. pēykkuk kaḷ vārttatu pōla.
Like pouring out toddy to a demon.
4955. pēykku vēppili pōlē.
As margosa leaves before a demon.
4956. pēykku vēli iṭṭatu pōla.
Like setting a demon to work.
4957. pēykkūttum āmaṇakkum āḷpōṉāl āḷ teriyātu.
A devil dance is a garden of castor oil plants, if one gets in he is not
seen agin.
4958. pēy cirittālum ākātu aḻutālum ākātu.
If a demon smiles it is bad, and if he weep that too is inuspicious.
4959. pēy piṭikkavum piḷḷi piḻikkavumā?
Will a child struck by a demon survive ?
4960. pēy pōyp puḷiyamarattil ēṟiṉatupōla.
As a demon ascended a tamarind tree.
4961. pērācikkāraṉip perumpuḷukāl vella vēṇṭum.
The avaricious must be overcome by notorious lies.
4962. pērāci periya naṣṭam.
Avarice ends in loss.
4963. pēr illāc caṉṉiti pāḻ, piḷḷi illāc celvam pāḻ.
The presence of one without reputation does no good, wealth with out a
child is useless.
4964. picācip paṇiyēl.
Yield not to a demon.
4965. pintamiḻp pulavōr pāṭṭukku ēṟṟavaṉ.
He is worthy to be sung by poets.
4966. piyac ceṉṟāl viyam tāṅkum.
If you walk gently, the earth will bear you.
4967. piya mitittatu vēṭaṉ aṭi , pataṟi mitittatu paṉṟi aṭi.
The light footstep is that of the hunter, the firm footstep is that of
the hog.
4968. piya meṉṟāl paṉiyiyum mellalām.
If masticated slowly even a palmyrah tree may be chewed.
4969. piyiṟ kaṭṭivitta poruḷ paṟikoṭukkappaṭṭatu.
The wealth tied up in a bag was lost.
4970. pokkavāycci mecciṉāḷām porimāvi.
It is sid that a toothless dame appreciated the rice flour.
4971. poṅkum kālam puḷi pūkkum, maṅkum kālam mā pūkkum.
In times of plenty the tamarind tree blossoms, in times of scarcity the
mango bears in abundance.
4972. poti aḷakkiṟataṟkumuṉṉē cattattiṟku aḷakkiṟatā?
Am I to measure out the hire, before measuring out the load?
4973. potiyi vittu viṭṭup piccikkup pōṉāṉ, atiyum vittu viṭṭuc cettuk
kiṭantāṉ.
Having loaded his bullock, he went abegging; the product he put on one
side, and died.
4974. poti vikkiṟataṟku muṉṉē cattattiṟku aḷakkiṟatā?
Am I to measure out the hire before adjusting the load?
4975. pottic curikkāy pōlē.
Like a fleshy gourd.
4976. poy iruntu pulampum mey iruntu viḻikkum.
Falsehood will never cease to weep, truth will ever be conspicuous.
4977. poy uṭi oruvaṉ col vaṉmiyiṉāl meypōlummēmeypōlummē.
The falsehood of a liar by reason of its force, may appear like truth,
may appear like truth.
4978. poy coṉṉa vāykkup pōcaṉam kiṭiyātu.
The mouth accustomed to lies will be deprived of food.
4979. poy coṉṉa vāykkup poriyum kiṭiyātu.
The mouth accustomed to lies will be deprived of oven parched corn.
4980. poy colli vāḻntavaṉum illi, mey collik keṭṭavaṉum illi.
No one ever prospered by telling lies, no onewas ever reduced to poverty
byspeaking truth.
4981. poy meyyi vellumā?
Will falsehood conquer truth ?
4982. poyyāṉa poruḷāci meyyāṉa aruḷāciyi vilakkukiṟatu.
The false love of money, will take away the real love of divine grace.
4983. poyyum oru pakkam poṟāmiyum oru pakkam.
Falsehood on one side, and envy on the other.
4984. poyyic coṉṉālum poruntac collavēṇṭum.
Though you tell lies, do so consistently.
4985. poruṭaṉip pōṟṟi vāḻ.
Preserve your substance and live.
4986. poruḷāciyum maṉaccāṭciyum poruntumā?
Will covetous desires and conscience agree?
4987. poruḷum koṭuttup paḻiyum tēṭa.
Giving one's wealth and incurring censure.
4988. poruḷum pōkamum kūṭa varātu puṇṇiyamē kūṭa varum.
Wealth and pleasure will be separated from us, but virtue will abide.
4989. poruḷ pōṉa vaḻiyē tukkam pōm.
Whithersoever wealth goos, sorrow follows in the same path.
4990. pollāta maṉam kēḷātu.
The wicked heart resists reproof.
4991. pollāṅku eṉpavi ellām tavir.
Avoid whatever is evil.
4992. pollāta kālam collāmal vantatu.
Inuspicious times come without giving notice.
4993. pollātavarkaḷ ciṉṉappaṭṭāl kalliṉ piḷavupōla rāciyākamāṭṭārkaḷ.
The anger of the wicked is like a fracture in a stone, they are not
easily reconciled.
4994. pollāta kuṇattukku maruntu uṇṭā?
Is there any medicine for a bad temper?
4995.pollātavarkaḷ caṅkāttam uppu maṇalil vīḻnta nīr pōla.
The friendship of the wicked is as bitter as water in brackish soil.
4996. pollāp piḷḷiyil illāp piḷḷi.
A bad child is worse than none.
4997. poḻutu paṭṭa iṭam viṭuti viṭṭa iṭam.
Halting where the sun sets.
4998. poḻutu viṭintatu pāvam tolintatu.
The day has dawned, sin is ended.
4999. poṟi veṉṟavaṉē aṟiviṉ kuruvām.
He alone who conquers his senses is a teacher of wisdom.
5000. poṟuti eṉpatu kaṭaliṉum peritu.
Forbearance is greater than the ocean.
5001. poṟuttal kacappāy iruntālum poṟukkap poṟukkat tittippu.
Although suffering may be bitter, continued endurance will make it
sweet.
5002. poṟuttār pūmi āḷvār.
Those who put up with injuries may rule the earth.
5003. poṟuttār aracāḷvār poṅkiṉār kāṭāḷvār.
They who endure will reign as kings; the irascible will wander through
the jungles.
5004. poṟumi puṇṇiyattiṟku vēr poruḷāci pāvattiṟku vēr.
Patient endurance is the root of religious merit, avarice is the root of
sin.
5005. poṟ kalam olikkātu veṇ kalam olikkum.
A gold vessel does not sound, a brass one does.
5006. poṟ kāppukku ācippaṭṭup puliyiṉ kiyil akappaṭpatupōla.
Like one whose desire for a gold bracelet, hurried him into the claws of
a tiger.
5007. poṟ pūviṉ vācaṉiyum murukkam pūviṉ vācaṉiyum cari.
The smell of a flower of gold and that of the murukku flower are alike.
5008. poṟ pū vācikkumā?
Does a golden flower diffuse fragrance?
5009. poṉ āparaṇattip pārkkilum pukaḻ āparaṇamē peritu.
The ornament of reputation is greater than ornaments of gold.
5010. poṉ iraval uṇṭu, pū iraval uṇṭā?
Gold may be lent, can flowers?.
5011. poṉ eṉṟāl piṇamum vāy tiṟakkum.
If the word gold be uttered, even a corpse will open its mouth.
5012. poṉ katti eṉṟu kaḻuttu aṟuttukkoḷḷalāmā?
May one cut his throat with a knife becuse it is made of gold?
5013. poṉ kātta pūtam pōlē.
Like the demon that guarded treasure.
5014. poṉ ceruppu āṉālum kālukkuttāṉ pōṭavēṇṭum
Though golden slippers, they must be put on the feet.
5015. poṉmaṇi aṟṟavaḷi ammaṇi eṉpāṉēṉ?
Why should a woman who has no gold beads be called Ammani?
5016. poṉmuṭi allatu caṭi muṭi vēṇṭum.
One should wear either a gold crown, or matted hir.
5017 poṉṉampalam uṇṭāṉāl eṉṉa ampalam kiṭiyātu ?
If one has a golden house, what house can he not get ?
5018. poṉṉampalattukkum puvaṉakirip paṭṭaṇattukkum eṉṟikkum uṇṭāṉa iḻavu.
The golden hall of-Chilambaram, and the town Puvanagiri, are always in
trouble.
5019. poṉṉāṅkāṇikkup puḷi viṭṭu ākkiṉāl uṇṇākku ellām tittikkum.
If acid be mixed with ponnankani-illecebrum sessile-its flavour will be
agreeable to the whole palate.
5020. poṉṉāṅkāṇikkup puḷi viṭṭu ākkiṉāl uṇṇāp peṇṇum oru uḻakku uṇṇum.
The compound of ponnankani greens-illecebrun-and tamarind, will enable a
girl who has lost her appetite to eat an ulak of rice.
5021. poṉṉāṉa maṉatip puṇṇākkukiṟāṉ.
He ulcerates the golden mind.
5022. poṉṉālē kalam uṇṭāṉālum maṇṇālē cuvar vēṇṭum.
Though one may possess vessels of gold, the wall of his house must be of
mud.
5023. poṉṉālē marumakaḷāṉālum maṇṇālē oru māmi vēṇṭum.
Though the dughter-in-law is made of gold, she must have a
mother-in-law made of earth.
5024. poṉṉiṉ kalappi varakukku uḻap pōṉatām varaku cērukku varaku paṭṭatām. Golden
ploughs were used for the cultivation of the millet, and the crop was
less than the seed-corn.
5025. poṉṉiṉ kuṭattiṟkup poṭṭu iṭṭup pārkka vēṇṭumā?
Must a royal mark be inscribed on a golden pot, that it may appear the
more beautiful?
5026. poṉṉiṉ kuṭam uṭintāl poṉ ākum eṉṉa ākum maṇṇiṉ kuṭam uṭintākkāl ?
Though broken to pieces a golden pot will still be gold, of what use is
an earthen pot when broken?
5027. poṉṉi vikkiṟa kōyililē pūviyāvatu vikkavēṉṭum.
Flowers, at least, must be offered in a temple in which gold is offered.
5028. poṉṉi eṟintālum poṭik kīriyi eṟiyalāmā?
Although you may throw away gold, you may not throw away edible herbs?
5029. pōkā ūrukku vaḻi kēḷ..
Inquire the way to the village whither you are not going.
5030. pōkāta iṭattilē pōṉāl varāta coṭṭu varum.
If you go where you ought not, you will receive a cuff that might have
been avoided.
5031. pōkkaṇam keṭṭavaṉ rājāvilum periyavaṉ.
An impudent person is greater than a king.
5032. pōkku aṟṟa nāykku pōṉatu ellām vaḻi.
A hungry dog finds a way wherever he goes.
5033. pōcaṉam ciṟuttālum ācaṉam perukka vēṇṭum.
Though one's food be slight, the dish must be large.
5034. pōtakar collit taṭṭātē, pātakar illik kiṭṭātē.
Obey your religious teacher, approach not the house of the wicked.
5035. pōtakarukkē cōtaṉi miñcum.
A religious teacher meets with many temptations.
5036. pōtum eṉṟa maṉamē poṉ ceyyum maruntu.
A contented mind is a specific for making gold.
5037. pōril ūci tēṭiṉa campantam.
Akin to seeking a needle in a heap of straw.
5038. pōrik kaṭṭivittup pōṭṭup piccikkup pōvāṉēṉ?
Having stacked your corn, why go abegging?
5039. pōrōṭu tiṉkiṟa māṭṭukkup piṭuṅkip pōṭṭuk kaṭṭumā?
When an ox is accustomed to eat at a heap, will it be satisfied with a
handful?
5040. pōrttoḻil puriyēl.
Do not practise the art of war.
5041. pōr piṭuṅkukiṟavarkaḷ pūrakkaḷam cāṭukiṟavarkaḷi māṭṭukiṟārkaḷām.
It is sid that those who steal from a corn-stack, will frighten those
who glean stealthily.
5042. pōlikku oru poṉmaṇi kiṭittatām, atit tūkkak kaṇṇil toṭṭut toṭṭup
pārttatām.
It is sid that the hollow-headed woman obtined a gold bead, and that
she examined it when she was sleepy.
5043. pōli poṟukkap pōccām pūṉi kuṟukkē pōccām.
It is sid that the destitute went out to gather orts, and a cat went
across the path.
5044. pōṉakam eṉpatu tāṉ uḻittu uṇṭal.
That which one eats as the fruit of his own labour, is properly called
food.
5045. pōṉa curattip puḷi iṭṭu aḻittatupōla.
Like inviting a fever that has subsided, by giving acids.
5046. pōṉati niṉikkiṟavaṉ putti keṭṭavaṉ.
He lacks sense who broods over the past.
5047. pōṉa māṭṭit tēṭuvārum illi, mēytta kūli koṭuppārum illi.
There is no one to seek the lost cow, none to pay the hire of the
cowherd.
5048. puvattu eḻilkōḷ.
Be graceful as the moon.
5049. peḷavap perumi teyvac ceyalē.
The greatness of the ocean shows the work of God.
5050. peḷavam uṟṟatu ākki, cevvi aṟṟatu vāḻkki .
The body is like a bubble, one's present existence is not lasting.
5051. makatēvar āṭu iṭittup pēyum āṭukiṟatu.
When butted by a ram of Mahadevar, even the demon shakes its head.
5052. makaḷukkup putti collit tāy avacāri pōṉāḷām.
The mother, having given advice to her dughter, played the harlot.
5053. makaḷ cettāḷ tāy tikku aṟṟāḷ.
The dughter is dead, the mother is become destitute.
5054. makaḷ cettāṟ piṇam makaṉ cettāṟ cavam.
If the dughter die her remins are regarded as a pinam; if the son, his
corpse is a savam.
5055. makaṉ cettālum cākaṭṭum marumakaḷ koṭṭam aṭaṅkiṉāṟ pōtum.
No matter if my son should die, it will suffice if the arrogance of my
dughter-in-law is checked.
5056. makāmakam paṉṉiraṇṭu varuṣattiṟku oru vici.
Mahamaham festival is celebrated every twelfth year.
5057. makā mēruvic cērnta kākamum poṉṉiṟamām.
Even a crow if it arrive at Mahameru is sid to assume a golden hue.
5058. makā rājāviṉ kaliyāṇattil nīrākāram ney paṭṭa pāṭu.
At the marriage feast of the Maha Rajah, even cold rice water is rare as
ghee.
5059. makā laṭcumi paratēcam pōṉatu pōla.
As Maha Lakshmi went on a pilgrimage.
5060. makimi cuntari katavi oñcari.
Illustrious beauty, leave the door ajar.
5061. makimikku añciya marumakaṉē erumik kaṉṟik kollātē.
Thou modest son-in-law, do not kill the young buffaloa.
This was sid to a man by his mother-in-law. Wishing to appear to her a
small eater he stinted himself at his ordinary meals, and yet appeared
to flourish. Casting about for a solution of the mystery her attention
was called to the ill condition of a young buffaloa. Suspecting that her
son-in-law was the cuse of this, she resolved to watch his movements.
The following night she saw him emerge from his room and proceed to the
buffaloa, whose milk he exhusted, and returned to his own quarters. On
the morrow when he took his food she addressed him in the language of
the proverb.
5062. makimippaṭṭavaṉukku maraṇam, māṭṭukkārappiyaṉukkuc caraṇam.
Death to the distinguished, homage to the cowherd.
5063. makimiyilē oru peṇ kuvaḷiyilē vāḻukiṟāḷ, atil oru peṇ aṟuttuviṭṭu
aḻukiṟāḷ.
one matro flourishes in the water-lily, and one weeps bereft of her
marriage symbol.
5064. makkaḷ kaḷavum varaku patarum cari.
The dishonest tricks of children, and the husks of varagu are alike.
5065. makkaḷ cōṟu tiṉṟāl makimi kuṟiyum.
To be fed by children is a disgrace to parents.
5066. makkāvukkup pōyk kokkup piṭittatu pōla.
Like going on a pilgrimage to Mecca, and catching a crane.
5067. maṅkamāri vantār taṅka maḻi peytatu.
Montgomery came, and it rined fine gold.
5068. maṅkum kālam māṅkāy poṅkum kālam puḷiyaṅkāy.
In time of scarcity, mangoes, and in a season of plenty, tamarind fruit,
are abundant.
5069. maṅki tīṭṭāṉāl kaṅkiyilē muḻukuvāḷ kaṅki tīṭṭāṉāl eṅkē muḻukuvāḷ?
If the damsel is polluted she may be cleansed by the ganges, but if the
ganges be polluted whither can she go?
5070. maccattiṉ kuñcukku ippaṭi eṉṟāl, mātāvukku eppaṭiyō?
If such be the condition of the young fish, what will be that of the
mother?
5071. maccāṉip pārkka uṟavum illi, mayirip pārkkak kaṟuppum illi .
No friendship superior to that of a cousin, nothing blacker than hir.
The word maccāṉ is used for a brother-in-law, and also for a maternal
uncle's son.
5072. maccāṉ cettāl mayir pōccu kampaḷi metti namakku āccu .
If my brother-in-law die I care not a hir, his cumbly mattress will be
mine.
5073. macci aḻittāl kuccukkum ākātu.
If the roof be destroyed, the house will not answer for a hut.
5074. mañcaṉamum malarum koṇṭu tutikkāviṭṭālum neñcakattil niṉippatē pōtakam.
Though one may not worship god by bathing him, and scattering flowers on
him, we are tught to keep him in mind.
5075. maṭakkiḻavaṉilum putti uḷḷa vālipaṉ atikam.
A prudent youth is superior to a stupid old man.
5076. maṭak kēḻvikku māṟuttaram illi.
A stupid question needs no answer.
5077. maṭap perumiyē tavira nīrākārattukkum vaḻi illi.
Besides a choultry, there is nothing - not even water.
5078. maṭam piṭuṅkik koṇṭu pōkumpōtu nantavaṉattukkuvaḻi eṅkē eṉkiṟāṉ.
After the choultry has been destroyed, he asks the way to the flower
garden.
5079. maṭi māṅkāy pōṭṭut tali veṭṭalāmā?
Are you about to behead one upon whom you have forced mango fruit?
5080. maṭiyilē kaṉam iruntāl vaḻiyilē payam.
If you have money in your waist-cloth, you may be afrid on the way.
5081. maṭiyip piṭittuk kaḷḷi vārttu mayirip piṭittuk kācu vēṇṭukiṟatā?
Having forced one to take toddy, do you seize him by the hir and demand
payment?
5082. maṭṭāṉa pōcaṉam maṉatiṟku makiḻcci.
Moderation in eating exhilerates the mind.
5083. maṭṭi erukkali maṭal maṭalāyp pūttālum, marukkoḻuntu vācaṉi varumā?
Though the erukkali bears bunches of flowers, has it the fragrance of
southern-wood-artemisia austriaca ?
5084. maṭṭu illāmaṟ koṭuttālum tiṭṭuk kēṭkal ākātu.
Though he gives liberally, it is not right to hear his abuse.
5085. maṭṭik kariyiyum maṭappaḷḷiyāriyum nampappaṭātu.
Palm-stem charcoal and Madappallis are not to be trusted.
5086. maṇaliṉ mēl viḻunta maḻittuḷi uṭaṉē maṟiyum; atu pōla, pollātavarkaḷukkuc
ceyta upakāram maṟintu pōm.
Drops of rin falling on sand will instantly disappear, in like manner
favours shown to the wicked will be soon forgotten.
5087. maṇaṟ cōṟṟil kal āyvatu pōla.
Like picking out stones when eating a dish of sand.
5088. maṇi nā aciyāmal irācciya pāram paṇṇukiṟatu.
So to govern a kingdom as not to move even the tongue of a bell.
5089. maṇ kutiriyi nampi āṟṟil iṟaṅkalāmā?
May one descend into a river relying on a mud horse ?
5090. maṇṭikkut takunta koṇṭi pōṭa vēṇṭum.
The knot of hir must be proportioned to the size of the head.
5091. maṇṭiyil eḻuti mayirāl maṟittatu pōla.
Like writing on the skull and covering it with the hir.
5092. maṇṭi uḷḷavariyil caḷi pōkātu.
As long as the head remins phlegm will abide.
5093. maṇṇāyiṉum maṉi āyiṉum kāppāṟṟiṉavarkaḷukku uṇṭu.
They may have house and grounds who know how to take care of them.
5094. maṇṇāṅkaṭṭi māppiḷḷikku erumuṭṭip paṇikāram.
Dried cow-dung is the proper form of wedding cake, when the bridegroom
is made of sand.
5095. maṇṇāl āṉāy maṇṇāy irukkiṟāy.
Made of earth, thou art earthy.
5096. maṇṇiṉmēl niṉṟu peṇ ōram collātē.
Whilst on earth pervert not judgment, in the case of a woman.
5097. maṇṇit tiṉṟālum maṟiyat tiṉ.
Though what you may eat be sand, eat it in a secluded place.
5098. maṇṇōṭē piṟakkalām uṉṉōṭē piṟantatil.
It were better to be born of the earth, than to be your brother.
5099. maṇ paṟittu uṇṇēl.
Do not live by extortion.
5100. maṇ piḷḷi āṉālum taṉ piḷḷi.
Though earthen, one's own child is precious.
5101. maṇ pūṉi āṉālum elip piṭittāl cari.
Though the cat is made of mud, if it catch rats, it is enough.
5102. maṇveṭṭi kūtal aṟiyumā?
Is a hoe sensible of cold?
5103. maṇvēliyō puṇvēliyō?
Are you engaged in making earthen ware, or sores ?
5104. matalikku illik kītamum aṟivum.
A child has neither the power of singing nor discretion.
5105. mataṉa malikku oppiṭalām.
He is comparable to Mathana mountin.
5106. matāpimāṉam cucātimāṉam tēcāpimāṉam.
Love of father, caste, and country.
5107. matiyāta vācalil mitiyātiruppatē uttamam.
It is not well to tread even on the threshold of a house in which you
are not respected.
5108. matiyum umatu vitiyum umatu.
Thy purpose and thy destiny.
The settled judgment, and the decrees of God, are in harmony.
5109. matiyi mīṉ cūḻntatu pōla.
As the stars surround the moon.
5110. matil mēl irukkiṟa pūṉi pōl irukkiṟāṉ.
He is like a cat on a wall.
Spoken of one who makes the most of his position.
5111. matu pintu kalakam pōl irukkiṟatu.
Like the uproar of a honey drop.
5112. maturikku vaḻi vāyil irukkiṟatu.
The way to Madura is in the mouth.
5113. mantiram kāl mati mukkāl.
The incantation is one fourth, and common sense three fourths.
5114. mantirattil māṅkāy viḻumā?
Will mangoes fall by a charm ?
5115. mantiram illāṉ pūci anti paṭumaḷavum.
Religious ceremonies not regulated by a form, will continue till
sun-set.
5116. mantirikku aḻaku varum poruḷ urittal.
It is the attribute of a minister to foretell things likely to occur.
5117. mantiri illā yōcaṉiyum āyutam illāc cēṉiyum keṭum.
Decisions without councillors, and troops without arms, will perish
5118. mantiyilum pāl vīṭṭilum tayirā?
Do you expect milk in tħe fold, and curds at home?
5119. mayir uḷḷa cīmāṭṭi vāri muṭikkiṟāḷ.
The damsel who has a fine head of hir, combs and dresses it.
5120. mayir ūṭāṭātār naṭpuc ciṟitu poruḷ ūṭāṭak keṭum.
Friendship so close that a hir cannot be intruded between the parties,
will be destroyed if money matters interpose.
5121. mayir cuṭṭuk kariyākiṟatā?
Can charcoal be formed by burning hir?
5122. mayir piḷakka vakitēṭiṉāṟpōla.
Like seeking means to split a hir.
5123. mayilāppūr ēri uṭittuppōkiṟatu eṉṟāl, varukiṟa kamiṭṭikku ākaṭṭum
eṉṟāṟpōla.
Like saying it may be deferred to the next committee, on hearing that
the Mylapore tank has given way.
5124. mayilē mayilē eṉṟāl iṟaku koṭukkumā?
If you exclim O peacock, O peacock, will it give you its feather?
5125. mayiṟ kaṇṇikku macakki, māppiḷḷikku avasti.
The peacock-eyed bride is phrensied, her bridegroom is in anguish.
5126. maraṇattilum keṭṭa mārkkattukkup payappaṭu.
Be more afrid of a vicious course than of death.
5127. maraṇattiṟku vaḻi maṭṭilli.
There are endless ways that lead to death.
5128. marattil iruntu viḻuntavaṉi māṭu mitittatu pōla.
As an ox trampled under foot a man that bad fallen from a tree.
5129. marattup paḻam marattu aṭiyilē viḻum.
The fruit of a tree will fall at its foot.
5130. maratti ili kākkum māṉattip paṇam kākkum.
Leaves cover a tree, money covers one's nakedness.
5131. maranāyilē puḻuku vaḻikkalāmā?
Can you obtin musk from a polecat?
5132. maram taṉṉi veṭṭukiṟavaṉukku niḻal koṭukkum.
The tree affords shade to the man who is felling it.
5133. maram veṭṭukiṟavaṉukku niḻalum, maṇ tōṇṭukiṟavaṉukku iṭamumkoṭukkum.
The tree affords shade to him who fells it, the earth supportshim who
digs it.
5134. maram ēṟik ki viṭṭavaṉum, kaṭaṉ vāṅkik kaṭaṉ koṭuttavaṉum keṭṭāṉ. He who let
go his hold after climbing a tree, and he who borrowed money to lend,
came to grief.
5135. maram vittavaṉ taṇṇīr vārppāṉ.
He who planted the tree will water it.
5136. mariyāti illātāṉ makimi aṟṟāṉ.
He who was not respectful lost his reputation.
5137. mariyāḷ kuṭittaṉam cariyāyp pōccu.
Mary's domestic life has come to an end.
5138. maruṇṭavaṉ kaṇṇukku iruṇṭatellām pēy.
To a gloomy eye all obscure things are demons.
5139. maruttu māvukkuc cayikkiṉi, maṭaṅkāk kutirikkuc cavukkaṭi.
A sign to a fleet horse, whipping to an obstinate one.
5140. maruntu kāl mati mukkāl.
Medicine one fourth, Common sense three fourths.
5141. maruntum viruntum mūṉṟu nāḷ.
Three days for testing a medicine, and for a feast.
5142. maruntē āyiṉum viruntōṭu uṇ.
Though the quantity be little, like medicine, distribute before eating
it.
5143. marumakaṉukkeṉṟu camittati makaṉukku iṭṭu vayiṟu erintāḷām.
It is sid that what she cooked for her son-in-law she gave to her son,
and was distressed about it.
5144. maruvil niṉṟa cāppāṭṭi laṅkaṇattil niṉittukkoṇṭatu pōla.
Like remembering, when hungry, the food of the marriage feast.
5145. malaṭikkut teriyumā piḷḷiyip peṟṟa arumi?
Does the barren woman understand the pins of parturition ?
5146. malaṭi aṟivāḷā piḷḷi arumi?
Does the barren woman know the endearments of children?
5147. malaṭiyip piḷḷi peṟac coṉṉāl peṟuvāḷā?
If the barren woman be asked to bring forth a child, will she do so?
5148. malaril maṇamum, eḷḷil eṇṇeyum, uṭalil uyirum kalantatu pōla.
All pervading, like fragrance in a flower, oil in sesamum seed, and life
in the body.
5149. malinta paṇṭam kaṭiyilē varum.
When commodities are abundant, they come to market.
5150.mali attaṉi cuvāmikkut tiṉi attaṉi puṣpam.
A flower, as small as a millet-seed, is dedicated to an idol as large as
a mountin.
5151. mali attaṉi cuvāmikku mali attaṉi puṣpam pōṭukiṟārkaḷā?
Do they dedicate flowers as large as mountins, to idols as large a
mountins?
5152. mali ilakkāṉāl kuruṭaṉum eyvāṉ.
If a mountin be the target, even a blind man may shoot.
5153. mali ucciyil kal ēṟṟutal aritu.
It is difficult to roll a stone to the top of a hill.
5154. mali ēṟiṉālum mittuṉaṉik ki viṭātē.
Though you ascend the mountins, do not leave behind your
brother-in-law, or the son of your maternal uncle.
5155. malittēṉ muṭavaṉukku varumā?
Will mountin honey come to the lameman?
5156. mali nellikkāykkum kaṭal uppukkum uṟavu ceytavar ār?
Who created the affinity between the mountin nelli, fruit, and sea-salt
?
5157. malipōlap pirāmaṇaṉ pōkiṟāṉām, piṉ kuṭumikkuaḻukiṟāḷām.
It is sid that when a brahman who was equal to a mountin was dying,
his wife was weeping for his tuft of hir.
5158. mali pōla vantatellām paṉipōla nīṅkum.
All that has come upon thee like mountins, shall pass away as dew.
5159. mali muḻaṅkic cuṇṭeli peṟṟatupōla.
As a mountin amidst thunder brought-forth a mouse.
5160. malimēl iruppārip paṉṟi pāyvatu uṇṭā?
Can the wild hog rush on thesewho are on the mountin top?
5161. maliyil viḷintālum uralil maciyavēṇṭum.
Although produced on the mountins, the rice must be prepared for use in
a mortar.
5162. maliyē maṇṇāṅkaṭṭi ākiṟapōtu, maṇṇāṅkaṭṭi eppaṭiākum?
When a mountin becomes a sod, what will the sod be like ?
5163. maliyē viḻuntālum taliyē tāṅkavēṇṭum.
Should a mountin fall, the head must bear it.
5164. maliyik kalli eli piṭittatu pōla.
Like excavating a mountin and catching a rat.
5165. maliyic cuṟṟi aṭittavaṉic ceṭiyic cuṟṟi aṭiyēṉā?
After having chased and beaten him round a mountin, will it be
difficult to do so round a bush?
5166. maliyit tuḷikkac ciṟṟuḷi pōtātā?
Is not asmall chisel sufficient to perforate a rock?
5167. maliyit tuḷikka vācci uḷi vantāṟpōla.
As if an adze and chisel came to perforate the rock.
5168. maliyip pārttu nāy kulittāl malikkuk kēṭō nāykkuk kēṭō?
If a dog bark at a mountin, will the mountin be injured or the dog?
5169. maliyi mali tāṅkum maṇṇāṅkaṭṭi tāṅkumā?
A rock supports a rock, can a sod do so ?
5170. mali viḻuṅki ammiyārukkuk katavu cuṇṭāṅkiyā?
Will a door be a difficulty to my aunt who has swallowed a mountin?
